CÁCH ĐỌC TỆP NÀY
- Mỗi tệp có một khối riêng với đường dẫn tương đối bên trong ZIP và đường dẫn đầy đủ sau khi giải nén.
- Tệp văn bản: toàn bộ nội dung được đặt giữa BEGIN FILE CONTENT và END FILE CONTENT.
- Tệp nhị phân (ví dụ PNG): dữ liệu được mã hóa Base64 để vẫn lưu được trong TXT; giải mã Base64 sẽ khôi phục byte gốc.
- SHA-256 và kích thước gốc được ghi kèm để có thể kiểm tra tính toàn vẹn.
- Các đường dẫn dùng dấu / theo chuẩn ZIP; thư mục gốc sau giải nén là tên tệp ZIP bỏ phần .zip.

# Veil Profile Studio Universal — v3.1.0

Veil is a local, opt-in Chromium profile/privacy layer. It aligns selected UA and User-Agent Client Hints values across HTTP and JavaScript, supports persistent profile definitions and site assignment, and offers deterministic best-effort read-back hardening for some graphics/audio/geometry APIs. The optional desktop companion launches separate browser user-data directories and can configure an HTTP(S) upstream proxy per launched instance.

**This software is not undetectable. No CAPTCHA, anti-bot, anti-fraud, or managed-policy bypass is provided.** Each surface has an explicit coverage state, and a test is only called live-verified when it ran in a real compatible browser environment. No surface should be treated as covered just because a toggle exists.

## Packages

- `Veil_Profile_Studio_Universal.zip`: one portable bundle containing the adaptive MV3 extension, desktop launcher, source, tests and installation guide.
- `Veil_Profile_Studio_PC.zip`: clean PC MV3 extension payload, with `manifest.json` at ZIP root. Load it unpacked only in browsers that support that flow.
- `Veil_Profile_Studio_PC_MV3.zip`: target-labelled compatibility archive retained for existing workflows.
- `Veil_Profile_Studio_Android_Installer.zip`: Android-labelled extension payload ZIP with `manifest.json` at the archive root. It is **not a universal Android installer**: use it directly only if the browser explicitly supports importing extension ZIPs; otherwise extract it and use that browser’s supported local/unpacked-extension flow.
- `Veil_Profile_Studio_Android_MV3.zip`: equivalent target-labelled Android package for manual extraction/loading.
- `Veil_Profile_Studio_Desktop_Launcher.zip`: optional Node.js 20+ desktop-only CLI/local UI; it creates one browser user-data directory per profile and embeds the profile definition + seed into a profile-specific extension build.
- `Veil_Profile_Studio_MV3.zip`: generic MV3 extension.
- `Veil_Profile_Studio_MV2_Legacy.zip`: legacy header-only candidate for genuinely MV2-capable browsers. Standard Chromium 139+ should use MV3 instead.
- `Veil_Profile_Studio_Source.zip`: full source/tests/build scripts for this release.

Install guides: [`docs/PC_INSTALL.md`](docs/PC_INSTALL.md), [`docs/ANDROID_INSTALL.md`](docs/ANDROID_INSTALL.md), and [`docs/VERIFICATION_RUNNER.md`](docs/VERIFICATION_RUNNER.md). The PC and Android packages are labels around the same adaptive MV3 runtime, not different browser engines. The Universal ZIP is a container, not an extension installer. On Android, extract `android-install/Veil_Profile_Studio_Android_Installer.zip`; **do not assume tapping a ZIP installs it**. Only choose an “Import extension ZIP” action if the exact browser build documents that feature. If it only supports “Load unpacked”, extract the installer fully and select the extracted directory containing `manifest.json`. If the browser offers neither local flow nor a supported store listing, installation is unsupported there; do not disable browser protections to force it. On PC, extract `Veil_Profile_Studio_PC.zip` and use the browser’s unpacked-extension flow; use `launcher/` only on desktop. Runtime capability detection remains conservative; the bundle does not spoof a physical device or make unsupported surfaces available. Android does not include or run the desktop companion.

## Install the extension

1. Extract the target ZIP.
2. Open the browser extension manager and use its supported local/unpacked-extension installation flow.
3. Open Veil's Options page. The extension starts disabled on a fresh install; enable profile controls explicitly.
4. Select an appropriate profile and review its `Coverage & limits` status. Reload open tabs after changes to page-world patches.

Some branded Google Chrome builds restrict `--load-extension`; the desktop launcher runs `veil doctor` against the binary you supply and refuses to claim a launcher-configured session when extension loading is not verified. It does not bypass browser policy. Android users need a browser that actually supports extension installation. Standard Google Chrome for Android does not install desktop Chrome extensions; ZIP/CRX packaging cannot change that platform limitation. On extension-capable browsers, use only their documented local/store installation flow. The upstream Kiwi README says Kiwi is no longer maintained after January 2025; its behavior and API support must be checked against the exact installed build. No Android device test is implied by a desktop Chromium test.

## Profile modes

Profiles are persistent objects stored under the independent key `veilProfileStudioProfileLibraryV1` (profile-library schema 1). Existing settings remain under `veilProfileStudioSettingsV1` (settings schema v2); existing base profile IDs are retained. Profile-definition exports are versioned and never include secrets.

- **identity** — UA and UA-CH profile behavior, subject to runtime capability and cross-OS policy.
- **hardened** — seeded, deterministic best-effort changes to selected Canvas, WebGL, Audio Analyser and text/rectangle read-back values. These implementations are partial and may affect visual output or applications.
- **full** — enables the hardened set plus the implemented locale/timezone and user-supplied geolocation hooks where available. The label means the fullest requested configuration, **not complete fingerprint coverage**. Unsupported fields remain native and are shown as uncovered.

A seed belongs to a profile and remains stable across calls/reloads while that profile definition is unchanged. Cloning creates a different seed. Profiles with a duplicate UA/template can still differ in selected deterministic read-back values; seeded changes are not a claim of a genuine physical device.

The bundled catalogue keeps existing profile IDs. Template provenance records source/confidence where supplied; fields without verified compatible device data are deliberately left native/unavailable rather than invented. A rule-based consistency score is a diagnostic heuristic, not an anonymity probability.

## Desktop launcher (optional)

Requires Node.js 20 or newer; the launcher uses only Node built-ins and has no native or third-party runtime dependencies. It does not download browsers. Run `npm run build` once from the source root to validate the release and stage a self-contained `launcher/project` tree, then install the supplied `launcher/` package locally:

```sh
npm install --global ./launcher
veil config browser /absolute/path/to/chromium
veil doctor
veil profiles create work --template chrome-linux --mode hardened --name "Work"
veil profiles assign work example.test
veil launch work
```

On Windows, run `npm install --global .\launcher` from the project directory in PowerShell and supply an absolute path to the browser executable. Run `veil help` for all commands. `VEIL_HOME` can select another absolute launcher-data directory. Each profile gets its own launcher-owned `profiles/<id>/user-data-dir` and matching extension build. Cookies, Local Storage, IndexedDB, Cache Storage and Service Worker registrations are kept in that browser data directory; a cloned definition gets a fresh directory, not copied sessions.

`veil doctor` verifies selected switches against the supplied browser using temporary profiles and loopback-only pages. Only tested switches are used for a launch. If browser execution or extension loading is blocked, `veil launch` fails closed rather than reporting fake coverage. The launcher does not pass any `--remote-debugging-*` flag and does not offer Selenium, Puppeteer, Playwright orchestration or a remote-control endpoint.

Proxy URLs must not embed credentials. The launcher supports HTTP(S) upstream proxy endpoints through a loopback relay for each launched profile. Proxy credentials live in an AES-256-GCM encrypted vault using a passphrase-derived key (scrypt); secrets are entered interactively and never exported/logged. Proxy-side DNS behavior depends on the upstream proxy. SOCKS proxies are not currently supported by the relay. The optional “match proxy exit” action is user-triggered and queries only a Geo-IP endpoint the user configured; no third-party endpoint is hard-coded.

The local UI binds to `127.0.0.1`, uses a random per-run token, checks Host and Origin, serves self-hosted files only, and has no CORS. Do not share the printed tokenized URL.

## Permissions and local data

The MV3 required permissions remain `storage`, `scripting`, `declarativeNetRequest`, `webNavigation`, and `alarms`; `privacy` is optional. The extension asks for optional privacy permission only after a user requests its WebRTC policy. That policy, when supported, is browser-wide—not per-profile and not a proxy guarantee. Chromium does not permit the `proxy` permission to be declared optional; the extension does not claim to use it. Per-instance proxy is a launcher feature.

The extension does not use remote code, telemetry, analytics, `eval`, or `new Function`. Extension self-tests are offline. The companion's only outbound network activity is the configured proxy traffic from the browser it launches and an optional user-triggered request to a user-configured Geo-IP endpoint through that proxy. The companion UI itself makes no unsolicited external requests.

Settings and profile exports contain definitions only. The launcher export excludes cookies, sessions, browser storage, proxy credentials and vault contents. Extension-only storage snapshot swapping is **not implemented and is not isolation**; it cannot isolate concurrent profiles or all IndexedDB/cache/Service Worker data.

## Real-time extension-state synchronization (v2.1.7)

Popup and Options now use a single background-owned settings state with a durable monotonic revision (`veilProfileStudioStateRevisionV1`). Each supported write carries the revision it was based on. A stale write receives `STATE_CONFLICT` instead of silently overwriting a newer save. Both MV3 and the legacy MV2 UI publish committed changes to other extension views; a clean view reloads the committed settings, while a dirty form is preserved until the user saves or resolves the conflict with the explicit **Discard edits & load latest** action.

Both MV3 and MV2 backgrounds reconcile direct `chrome.storage.local` changes to the settings key through the serialized operation queue. Bursts are coalesced by reading the latest persisted value, schema validation is strict, invalid changes are reverted to the last-known-good settings, and reconciliation outcomes increment the revision and publish sanitized notifications. MV2 also reconciles its rotation alarm on valid external writes but remains a network-header-only compatibility path.

**“Real-time” here means extension settings and diagnostics views, not live rewriting of already-running web pages.** Previously loaded page realms are not hot-patched; navigate or reload the test page after changing page-world profile settings. Worker contexts, WebGPU/GPU identity, physical device properties, operating-system settings, and the exact headers received by a server remain subject to the coverage matrix below. The settings schema and existing profile IDs are unchanged.

## Cross-context consistency diagnostics (surface set introduced in v2.1.6)

See [the surface audit](docs/FINGERPRINT_SURFACE_AUDIT.md) for the coverage matrix and limitations.

The local self-test now compares the page with Dedicated Worker and (when supported) SharedWorker values, checks Service Worker API/controller presence, and probes WebGL renderer-parameter readability, WebGPU adapter availability, pointer/hover media-query coherence, screen/DPR/viewport sanity, Canvas-versus-OffscreenCanvas pixel consistency, repeated offline-audio rendering, plugin/MIME collection shape, and Date/Intl API sanity. Its report contains only fixed keys and categorical statuses; raw values stay in the page and are not exported. It sends no network requests.

These are **diagnostics, not masking**. Worker and ServiceWorker contexts remain native/uncontrolled; API availability does not mean a surface is protected. Full GPU identity, hardware identity, OS-level locale/timezone, physical display, all audio/rendering behaviors, and the actual `Accept-Language` header received by a server are not guaranteed to match a selected profile. `unavailable` is not `PASS`. Do not use a local smoke report as evidence of a real extension install or a successful test on your website.

## Coverage & limitations

| Surface/context | Extension only | Extension + desktop launcher |
|---|---|---|
| UA + low-entropy UA-CH on registered documents/HTTP | Covered only when the browser APIs register/apply successfully | Same, with profile-specific static MAIN-world bundle when `doctor` verifies extension loading |
| High-entropy UA-CH | Partial: same-OS OS/device values remain native; cross-OS values are synthesized for selected fields | Same; launcher cannot change the browser engine's underlying brand/version implementation |
| HTTP vs JS Client Hints | Partial: selected headers/fields are aligned; DNR cannot condition a header change on header presence or `Accept-CH` | Same |
| Same-/cross-origin iframes at document start | Covered where registration matches the frame and permissions allow it; opaque/dynamic `about:blank` cases may be early-read races | Same; static bundle selects a profile by hostname at document start for registered pages |
| Dynamically created `about:blank` iframe / popup synchronously read before injection | Partial / race-dependent | Partial / race-dependent |
| Dedicated Worker, SharedWorker | Uncovered/native; worker constructors are not rewritten because safe rewriting cannot preserve all CSP/module/blob/data/relative-import cases | Uncovered/native; no unverified switch is claimed to fix this |
| Service Worker / WorkerNavigator / OffscreenCanvas worker surfaces | Uncovered/native | Uncovered/native; isolated user-data-dir does not spoof Service Worker APIs |
| Canvas read-back | Partial, selected APIs only | Partial, selected APIs only; static profile bundle can run earlier but not add unsupported API coverage |
| WebGL readPixels and selected numeric caps | Partial; renderer/vendor/precision and many parameters remain native | Same |
| WebGPU adapter, full GPU identity | Uncovered | Uncovered |
| AudioContext | Partial; selected AnalyserNode read-back only; audio buffers/offline behavior remain native | Same |
| ClientRects/TextMetrics | Partial; selected methods only, with potential small layout/measurement changes | Same |
| CPU cores/RAM, touch/pointer, plugins/MIME, media devices, Network Information | Uncovered/native | Uncovered/native; switches do not produce a verified coherent mobile device |
| Screen/DPR/media queries/window metrics | Extension spoofing uncovered; launcher can set initial window-size/DPR only if `doctor` confirms the result | Partial engine-level control; physical screen/viewport and all media queries are not guaranteed to match |
| Locale, Accept-Language and timezone | Partial; JS locale/timezone paths and request-language overrides are not equivalent to changing the host OS | Partial; `--lang` and `TZ` are used only when live probe confirms them |
| Geolocation | Partial; only user-supplied coordinates and JS hooks where supported; this is not a physical-location change | Partial; no system location change is claimed |
| WebRTC/IP | Optional, browser-wide `chrome.privacy` policy if granted/supported; not a per-profile IP mask | Partial; WebRTC engine switch remains unverified and is not passed |
| Cookies, Local Storage, IndexedDB, Cache, Service Worker storage | Shared browser profile; no isolation | Separate user-data-dir per launcher profile; isolation must pass the local E2E test |
| DNS/proxy | Extension cannot configure DNS or optional browser-wide proxy permission | HTTP(S) upstream proxy per launcher instance; proxy-side DNS depends on upstream; SOCKS not supported |
| TLS JA3/JA4, HTTP/2, TCP/IP, header order | OUT | OUT |

### Explicitly out of scope

No TLS-interception/MITM proxy, custom root CA, custom network stack, TLS/JA3/JA4 spoofing, HTTP/2 fingerprint manipulation, TCP/IP stack spoofing or header-order spoofing. No CAPTCHA/challenge/vendor-specific logic, automation-flag hiding, account/session transfer or external session imports. A profile is a local consistency/privacy layer, not an anti-bot bypass or guarantee of anonymity.

### Structural and compatibility limits

Native-callable wrapping uses Proxy delegation so the original target performs receiver validation and exceptions. This preserves many observable properties, but `Function.prototype.toString()` can still differ from true browser-native source, and hostile scripts can replace configurable descriptors or compare other realms. Removing the known Symbol manager/revert handle fixes that specific recovery path; it does not make every patch impossible to alter or detect.

Rotation is off by default. Frequent identity changes within one site session can be inconsistent. Timed rotation, when explicitly enabled, changes profile configuration for later navigations; already-open page realms may require reload. Disable/reload cannot undo values already copied into page variables or erase website storage.

## Build and verification

Requirements: Node.js 20+, Python 3 for packaging/E2E helpers; Playwright 1.57.0 is a test-only optional dependency. Extension runtime has no remote code, native dependencies or telemetry.

```sh
npm run check
npm test
npm run build
npm run test:browser-smoke
python3 -m pip install -r requirements-e2e.txt
python3 -m playwright install chromium
npm run test:e2e
```

The E2E suite uses loopback-only servers. It separately tests extension behavior and browser user-data-dir storage separation. It first checks managed policy configuration and reports explicit blockers without trying to override those policies. The extension suite uses Playwright's documented `chromium` channel for headless extension support when its browser is installed; `CHROMIUM_BIN=/absolute/path/to/chromium` is an explicit alternative, but must still pass live extension-load checks. A policy block, missing browser, startup failure or missing Playwright returns `BLOCKED`, never `PASS`. `npm run test:e2e` writes a machine-readable report to `dist/E2E_REPORT.json`. Passing unit tests do not substitute for live Chromium E2E, and desktop results do not certify Kiwi/Quetta Android.

## Versioning and migration

Product versions follow the repository rule: bug fixes increment the final component, compatible feature releases increment the middle component, and a major profile/data-model redesign increments the first component. Version `2.0.0` introduced the persistent profile/data model; `2.1.0` added the desktop companion; `2.1.1` improves E2E blocker diagnosis and adds a single universal bundle; `2.1.2` fixes the standalone Android installer layout; `2.1.3` adds real-Chromium offline script smoke coverage for generated MAIN-world profile scripts; `2.1.4` removes non-payload files from installer archives, corrects the standalone launcher's bundle-relative imports (verified by extracting the ZIP and running its CLI), stages the generated launcher project before source tests, adds release-artifact structural auditing and Android/PC/runner install guides, and prevents generated launcher staging files from being duplicated into the source archive; `2.1.5` added initial local cross-context diagnostics for page and Dedicated Worker values. `2.1.6` expands local checks to SharedWorker consistency (when supported), Service Worker API/controller presence, readable WebGL renderer parameters (status only), WebGPU adapter availability, pointer/hover media-query coherence, screen/DPR/viewport sanity, Canvas-versus-OffscreenCanvas read-back consistency, repeated offline-audio render stability, plugin/MIME collection shape, and Date/Intl timezone API sanity. `2.1.7` adds durable state revisions, stale-write rejection, popup/options change notifications, an explicit recovery action for dirty forms, safe handling of unsaved edits, and latest-value coalescing for direct MV3/MV2 settings-storage changes. These diagnostics do not rewrite Worker constructors or spoof hardware/GPU/display/audio identities; unsupported realms remain explicitly unavailable/native. No network requests are sent by the local self-tests, and an Accept-Language rule snapshot still does not prove the exact header received by your server. Browser-script smoke PASS does not upgrade a policy-blocked extension E2E run to PASS. Settings schema v2 and legacy profile IDs remain unchanged; the separate revision key does not require a settings schema bump. Build output includes `SHA256SUMS.txt`, the portable `Veil_Profile_Studio_Universal.zip`, a clean `Veil_Profile_Studio_Android_Installer.zip`, a clean `Veil_Profile_Studio_PC.zip`, and `Veil_Profile_Studio_Source.zip`. The source archive omits generated `launcher/project/` staging copies; `npm test` and `npm run build` recreate that folder before the launcher tests/package are run. The standalone launcher imports its shared libraries from this bundle-local generated folder.

# Veil Profile Studio — Android installation guide (v2.1.7)

## First: check that your browser actually supports extensions

This ZIP is an unpacked Chromium MV3 extension payload. It is **not an APK**, and the ZIP itself is not a universal Android installer. Chromium ancestry alone does not imply support for local extensions.

Use only a browser build whose own extension manager/documentation supports Manifest V3 and a local install method. Do not disable browser security, change a managed policy, or install an APK from an untrusted source to force compatibility.

## Method A — the browser explicitly supports importing an extension ZIP

1. Open the browser's extension manager and confirm its own help/documentation explicitly says it accepts a local extension ZIP.
2. Select `Veil_Profile_Studio_Android_Installer.zip` (not `Veil_Profile_Studio_Universal.zip`).
3. Complete the browser's normal permission/install prompts.
4. Open the extension manager and verify that **Veil Profile Studio 2.1.6** appears enabled.

If the browser doesn't document ZIP import, do not assume this method works.

## Method B — the browser supports “Load unpacked” / loading a local folder

1. Extract `Veil_Profile_Studio_Android_Installer.zip` completely into a normal folder, for example `Downloads/Veil_Profile_Studio_2.1.6/`.
2. Open that extracted folder. `manifest.json` must be directly inside it; don't select the original ZIP and don't select the outer Universal folder.
3. In the browser's extension manager, use its documented **Load unpacked** / local-folder option and select the extracted folder containing `manifest.json`.
4. Complete the permission prompt, then verify the extension is listed and enabled.

Android file pickers vary. If the browser cannot access a folder or does not offer local-folder loading, this method is unsupported by that build.

## If neither method exists

The browser does not expose a supported local install route for this package. Use a browser with documented MV3 extension support or an official store listing where the project is published. Do not try to bypass its restrictions.

Google Chrome for Android does not provide the desktop Chrome extension runtime. Its “Add to Desktop” flow installs an extension on desktop Chrome, not into the mobile browser.

## After installing

1. Open the extension's options page from the browser's extension manager.
2. Confirm settings explicitly; a fresh install starts disabled by design.
3. Test on a harmless page you control first. Confirm the extension shows no error in its own diagnostics, then try the target page.
4. If it fails, capture the exact browser name and version, extension-manager error text, and whether the extension appears enabled. Don't share cookies, credentials, full browsing history, or private headers.

An install success does not guarantee every API is available on Android, nor does it guarantee every website will accept profile changes. The extension detects several unsupported capabilities and may have reduced coverage on some browser builds.

----- END FILE CONTENT -----

# Changelog — Veil Profile Studio

## 3.1.0 — Architecture Overhaul, Strict Identity/Hardening Separation, Bug Fixes & Coherence Engine — 2026-10-14

- **Architecture Separation (B1, B2)**: Separated static document_start Identity Layer from dynamic onCommitted Hardening Layer. Static bundles now only override Navigator identity without static seeds or hardcoded mode=full.
- **Per-Site Deterministic Seeds (5.2)**: Implemented 128-bit cyrb128 hash + sfc32 PRNG deriving isolated seeds per eTLD+1 to prevent cross-site linking.
- **Removed Anti-Bot Deceptions (Section 2)**: Completely removed navigator.webdriver overrides, surface.webdriver check in self-test, and misleading bot-bypass claims.
- **Self-Test v2 (B3, F7)**: Fixed message router (handling RUN_PAGE_SELF_TEST & RUN_SELF_TEST), sanitized DOM rendering without raw innerHTML, and removed arbitrary 18-row truncate.
- **Truthful Surface Capabilities (B4)**: Synchronized PROFILE_SURFACES so geometry and mediaDevices are accurately designated as uncovered.
- **Pointer & Media Query Parser (B5)**: Fixed compound media query evaluation and eliminated broken Proxy on MediaQueryList.
- **Idempotent Audio Protection (B6)**: Applied WeakMap cache to AudioBuffer.prototype.getChannelData to prevent cumulative jitter.
- **Date & Timezone Synchronization (B7)**: Replaced fragile string parsing with Intl.DateTimeFormat#formatToParts + Date.UTC calculation.
- **Safe Viewport Behavior (B8)**: Viewport dimensions (window.innerWidth/innerHeight) are no longer forcibly overridden by default.
- **WebRTC Safe Callbacks & Cleanup (B9)**: Added legacy callback support to createOffer/createAnswer; mapped listeners in WeakMap so removeEventListener functions properly; defaulted browser-wide WebRTC policy to default_public_interface_only.
- **Geolocation Prototype Parity (B10)**: Geolocation position returns valid GeolocationPosition/GeolocationCoordinates prototypes without prompting native permissions when coordinates are configured.
- **Modern WebGL Masking (B11)**: Intercepted UNMASKED vendor/renderer only after WEBGL_debug_renderer_info query; updated macOS string to ANGLE Metal.
- **Hardware Memory Bounds (B12)**: Clamped deviceMemory to valid Chromium steps <= 8.
- **Removed Fake Battery API (B13)**: Cleanly unpatched BatteryManager to preserve native browser behavior.
- **Canvas Multi-Pixel Perturbation (B15)**: Bounded 128-bit multi-pixel perturbation skipping transparent pixels, covering OffscreenCanvas.convertToBlob.
- **DNR Header Alignment (B16)**: Standardized Accept-Language q-values and removed deprecated sec-ch-ua-full-version.
- **Scoped Session Clearing (B18)**: Origin data clearing never silently falls back to browser-wide clearing on error.
- **Complete i18n & Fixed Missing Keys (B19)**: 100% complete dictionaries for English and Vietnamese, added missing profileSeedError, added fallback resolution.
- **Build & CI Automation (B20)**: Introduced tools/build.mjs, check-i18n.mjs, verify-claims.mjs, unit test suite, and SHA256SUMS.txt.

## 3.0.0 — Unified Universal PC & Android Package — 2026-10-13

- Unified PC and Android extension payloads into a single adaptive codebase.
- Introduced 20 browser profiles spanning Chrome/Edge 139, 154, and 155 across Windows, macOS, Linux, and Android.
- Added Profile Library v1 with per-profile surface selection, mode options, and locale/timezone customization.
- Enhanced Declarative Net Request priority routing for target and initiator domain overrides.


## 2.2.1 — Worker stealth, Pointer & Viewport profile alignment, Timezone alias fix, and Scoped session cleaning — 2026-10-13

- Dedicated Worker & SharedWorker Stealth: Implemented stealth wrapper for `Worker` and `SharedWorker` constructors that injects an identity prelude into worker realms, masking `navigator.userAgent`, `platform`, `vendor`, `language`, `languages`, `hardwareConcurrency`, `deviceMemory`, `userAgentData`, `Intl.DateTimeFormat`, and `Date.prototype.getTimezoneOffset`. Prevents real host fingerprints from leaking through background workers.
- Vietnam Timezone Alias Resolution: Added canonical equivalence mapping in the self-test diagnostic engine for Vietnam timezones (`Asia/Saigon` and `Asia/Ho_Chi_Minh`), eliminating false-positive mismatch reports caused by V8/Chromium resolving `Asia/Ho_Chi_Minh` to `Asia/Saigon`.
- Mobile Pointer & Viewport Alignment: `pointer: coarse`, `pointer: fine`, `hover: none`, and `hover: hover` media queries are now dynamically aligned with the selected profile on `matchMedia` and `MediaQueryList.prototype.matches`. Viewport dimensions (`window.innerWidth`, `innerHeight`, `outerWidth`, `outerHeight`, `visualViewport`, and `document.documentElement.clientWidth/clientHeight`) now adjust according to profile to prevent desktop mouse/viewport leaks when using mobile profiles.
- Scoped Session Cleaning: Scoped the "Dọn session" (Clear browsing session) action so that it clears browsing data (cookies, storage, cache) only for the active tab's origin when triggered on a website, preserving active sessions on other sites. When no active website is open, data is cleared only for the current browser session (`since: sessionStartTime`), avoiding logging out of all accounts browser-wide.
- Self-Test Diagnostics Upgrade: Self-test now dynamically evaluates worker consistency and reports `stealth-protected`. OffscreenCanvas and Canvas readback consistency has been harmonized across contexts.


## 2.1.7 — Real-time settings synchronization and conflict safety — 2026-10-12

- Added durable settings revisions and optimistic concurrency checks to MV3 and the legacy MV2 message APIs. Concurrent writes based on the same revision cannot silently overwrite each other; stale writes return a fixed `STATE_CONFLICT` code.
- Popup and Options views receive `VEIL_STATE_CHANGED` notifications and refresh the committed state when clean. Dirty forms are preserved on screen; a stale save is rejected and cannot silently overwrite newer values. A visible **Discard edits & load latest** action resolves a conflict explicitly instead of leaving dirty forms stranded.
- Direct settings-storage writes in both MV3 and MV2 legacy are reconciled through the serialized background queue. Event bursts re-read the latest persisted snapshot instead of applying a stale captured value; strict-invalid data is reverted to the last-known-good setting and a sanitized diagnostic is broadcast. MV2 also refreshes the timed-rotation alarm after a valid external write.
- Added regression checks for simultaneous stale writers, invalid revision tokens, bursts of external storage changes, rollback of invalid settings, and diagnostic notifications. Current Node suite: 99 tests; Python suite: 15 tests.
- The synchronization is for extension state and diagnostics. It does not hot-patch JavaScript realms in already-loaded tabs, make Worker APIs controllable, or guarantee the values/header observed by a remote site. MV2 remains explicitly header-only. Strict extension E2E is still `BLOCKED` where browser policy prevents installation or loopback navigation.
- No settings schema or base profile IDs were changed; `veilProfileStudioStateRevisionV1` is a separate state-control key.

## 2.1.6 — Deeper local surface diagnostics — 2026-10-12

- Adds status-only SharedWorker cross-context checks when the browser permits a temporary local Blob worker.
- Adds Service Worker API/controller presence checks without probing or mutating the site's registered Service Worker.
- Adds WebGL renderer-parameter readability and WebGPU adapter-availability diagnostics without exporting GPU identifiers.
- Adds pointer/hover media-query exclusivity, screen/DPR/viewport sanity, Canvas-versus-OffscreenCanvas pixel read-back consistency, repeated OfflineAudioContext rendering, plugin/MIME collection shape, and Date/Intl API sanity checks.
- Expands regression tests for sanitized output and contradictory media-query states.
- Does not claim to spoof the above surfaces. Worker/ServiceWorker contexts and host hardware/GPU/display/audio identity remain native or partially covered. The local self-test sends no network requests and is not an anti-bot/CAPTCHA bypass.

## 2.1.5 — Cross-context consistency diagnostics — 2026-10-12

- Expanded the local self-test from one Worker UA check to compare Worker UA/platform against the selected profile and compare language, languages, hardwareConcurrency, deviceMemory, Intl timezone, and Date timezone offset across the page and a temporary Dedicated Worker.
- Added presence-only checks for hardware, pointer/touch, screen/DPR, WebGL, WebGPU, OffscreenCanvas, audio, plugins/MIME and Intl APIs. Only categorical statuses are returned; raw hardware, display, renderer, audio or locale values are not exported.
- Self-test reports remain honest: Worker values are still native/uncontrolled; canvas/WebGL/audio hardening remains partial and WebGPU, screen, device hardware and input APIs remain native/uncovered. No Worker constructor rewriting, GPU renderer spoofing, hardware spoofing or CAPTCHA/anti-bot bypass was added.
- Added regression tests for cross-context mismatches and report redaction. Settings schema, profile IDs, permissions and packaged extension APIs are unchanged.

## 2.1.4 — Clean installer payloads and stronger release audit — 2026-10-12

- Removed the extra `TARGET_README.txt` from the Android installer ZIP so the archive is only the extension payload and has `manifest.json` at its root. The install instructions now explicitly distinguish ZIP import from extract-and-load-unpacked flows; neither is claimed for browsers that do not expose it.
- Added the exact `Veil_Profile_Studio_PC.zip` and `Veil_Profile_Studio_Android_Installer.zip` deliverables, while keeping target-labelled compatibility packages.
- The source ZIP no longer contains the generated duplicate `launcher/project/` tree. That tree remains in the desktop launcher and Universal component where the launcher needs a self-contained runtime.
- Added a release audit for ZIP CRCs, root manifests, required manifest resources, local HTML/CSS/module references, version alignment, Universal nested-package identity, generated-file exclusions, and checksum coverage. The build now runs the Python test suite and release audit before publishing artifacts.
- No extension runtime behavior or permissions were changed. Live extension E2E remains `BLOCKED` where managed policy prohibits extension installation or loopback navigation; that result is not promoted to `PASS`.

## 2.1.3 — Offline Chromium runtime smoke and clearer release verification — 2026-10-12

- Added a separate smoke test that loads every generated MAIN-world profile script into a real Chromium `about:blank` page and checks core navigator, Canvas, DOM layout, Intl, and optional UAData API usability. It performs no external navigation, does not load the extension, and does not change browser policy.
- Added machine-readable and Markdown reports at `dist/BROWSER_SCRIPT_SMOKE_REPORT.json` and `.md`; the CI workflow runs this smoke even when strict extension E2E is blocked.
- Tightened source validation so the desktop launcher version must match the main package version.
- No extension runtime behavior, settings schema, profile IDs, required permissions, or data formats changed in this release. The strict extension E2E remains `BLOCKED` in environments whose managed policy forbids extension loading or loopback URLs; the offline smoke is not represented as a substitute.


## 2.1.2 — Android package layout and installability — 2026-10-12

- Fixed a packaging trap: the Universal ZIP is a bundle container and is not itself a direct extension-install ZIP.
- Added `Veil_Profile_Studio_Android_Install.zip`, with `manifest.json` at the archive root and no extra wrapper directory, for extension-capable Android browsers whose importer accepts ZIP packages.
- The Universal bundle now embeds that install ZIP and documents the difference between a container archive and an install package.
- Added build-time checks for root manifest placement, required extension resources, version consistency, and ZIP CRC integrity.
- Clarified that standard Chrome for Android cannot load desktop extensions and that the build must not override managed browser policy.
- No CAPTCHA, anti-bot, anti-fraud or managed-policy bypass was added. Live browser E2E remains `BLOCKED` when the environment's policies prohibit extension loading or loopback tests.

## 2.1.1 — Universal bundle and honest E2E preflight — 2026-10-12

- Added a single portable ZIP containing the adaptive MV3 extension, desktop companion, complete source tree and install guide; Android remains extension-only and the launcher remains desktop-only.
- Extension E2E now selects Playwright's documented extension-capable `chromium` channel by default rather than silently falling back to a system/headless browser. An explicit `CHROMIUM_BIN` remains supported and must pass live checks.
- Added fast, explicit managed-policy preflight for extension installation and loopback HTTP. The harness does not override policy, and all BLOCKED outcomes remain non-zero. Removed `VEIL_E2E_ALLOW_BLOCKED` so a blocked run cannot be turned into exit code 0 by an opt-in flag.
- Added richer storage E2E diagnostics and a machine-readable aggregate report at `dist/E2E_REPORT.json`.
- Added regression tests for policy blockers, extension-capable browser selection, version consistency, and universal package contents.
- Version bump 2.1.0 → 2.1.1 is a patch release for test reliability and portable packaging; profile/settings schemas, IDs, permissions and reserved-domain exclusions are unchanged.

## 2.1.0 — Persistent profile layer and desktop companion — 2026-10-12

- Added persistent profile definitions with stable seed, mode (`identity`, `hardened`, `full`), notes, template provenance, validation and versioned definition-only import/export. Existing settings key/schema and base profile IDs are preserved.
- Added best-effort seeded perturbations for selected Canvas, WebGL, Audio Analyser, ClientRects/TextMetrics and supported Intl/geolocation paths. Surface status stays `partial` or `uncovered` where implementation/parity is not complete; this is not full fingerprint coverage.
- Added the Node 20+ desktop `veil` companion with separate user-data-dir per profile, static profile/seed bundle at `document_start` when the browser supports the necessary extension APIs, verified-switch `doctor`, local-only UI, HTTP(S) upstream proxy relay, and AES-256-GCM passphrase vault.
- Added per-profile groups/tags/notes, clone/delete, site assignments, proxy reference, versioned definition import/export, explicit deletion confirmation, path/symlink safety, active-profile locking, Host/Origin/token protection, and no remote-debugging flags.
- Added loopback-only Playwright storage-isolation E2E for cookies, Local Storage, IndexedDB and Cache Storage across separate user-data directories.
- Corrected first-start storage logic so an existing profile library is not overwritten by a default initialization; merged single-surface edits instead of discarding unrelated surface flags.
- Updated profile UI to lock launcher-owned identity, seed, mode, site assignments and rotation in a statically bundled instance; users can still change domain allow/block rules and disable the extension.
- Version bump 2.0.0 → 2.1.0 is for the optional desktop companion and separate launcher packaging. Runtime switches that cannot be live-verified remain unclaimed; browser E2E is reported as `BLOCKED` when environment policy prevents extension loading.

- Version bump 1.3.3 → 2.0.0 justified by the persistent profile data model and user-visible mode/profile workflow redesign.


## 1.3.3 — MAIN-world handle removal and UA-CH consistency — 2026-10-12

- Removed the Symbol-keyed page getter manager and authorization-token bridge. No page-visible restore/reapply handle is published; open documents must reload after profile changes or disable.
- Wrapped native callable targets with Proxy apply traps to retain native receiver/argument validation, descriptor flags, names, lengths and own-key shape. Exact `Function#toString` source equality is not achievable with this technique and is explicitly tested as a residual limitation.
- Aligned HTTP browser-version high-entropy Client Hints with `navigator.userAgentData`; same-OS physical OS/device hints remain native and cross-OS profiles use their profile template. UI warns that DNR can send browser-version hints before `Accept-CH` opt-in.
- Updated the loopback E2E harness to compare available high-entropy HTTP headers with JavaScript UAData rather than expecting all such headers to be stripped. Worker remains native/uncontrolled because broad constructor rewriting is not safe across CSP/module/relative URL/SharedWorker cases.
- Built separate PC-labelled and Android-labelled MV3 ZIPs sharing the same adaptive runtime code, plus generic MV3, MV2 Legacy and source archive. Required permissions, settings key/schema, existing profile IDs, export compatibility and reserved-domain exclusions are unchanged.
- Patch version bumped from 1.3.2 to 1.3.3 for bug fixes and consistency corrections; no settings schema bump was needed.

### Verification boundary
- Node and Python tests, static guardrails, generated-profile VM checks and ZIP packaging are run for this release. Live E2E remains BLOCKED in this sandbox because Chromium headless does not launch reliably; no live HTTP/JavaScript PASS is claimed.

## 1.3.2 — E2E browser discovery and false-success fix — 2026-10-12

- E2E now prefers the installed Playwright Chromium build, honors `CHROMIUM_BIN`, and falls back to a local system Chromium/Chrome/Edge if the bundled Playwright browser is not installed. Missing or invalid executables return stable `BLOCKED` codes, not raw paths or exception text.
- Corrected `VEIL_E2E_ALLOW_BLOCKED=1`: it can relax the exit code only after a managed policy is positively identified. A missing service worker with no matching policy remains a failing run, preventing false CI success.
- Policy inspection now separately detects extension install blocking and `URLBlocklist` rules covering loopback HTTP. Only booleans are retained; policy file contents are never emitted.
- Added standard-library Python tests for browser launch option selection, policy classification, and exit-code semantics. `npm test` runs these in addition to Node tests; `npm run check` syntax-parses the Python harness and helpers. The local echo server opts into UA Client Hints and reports high-entropy removal only if the native baseline proves those hints were emitted.
- Runtime permissions, settings key, schema v2, export format and 20 profile IDs are unchanged. No new anti-bot evasion logic or native hardware/performance/network/time spoofing was added.

### Verification boundary

## 1.3.1 — E2E harness and packaging reliability — 2026-10-12

- Fixed a broken `test:e2e` entrypoint that referenced a non-existent `e2e_baseline.py` file. The source package now includes `tools/e2e.py` and `requirements-e2e.txt`.
- Added a Playwright persistent-context smoke test using a loopback-only HTTP echo server. It checks top-level document, script, iframe and fetch headers; main page/network iframe/about:blank/srcdoc JavaScript UA values; UAData presence; and expected native/uncontrolled Worker behavior. Raw UA/header values are never printed.
- The harness distinguishes `PASS`, `FAIL`, and `BLOCKED`; a managed policy that prevents extension activation is not counted as success. `VEIL_E2E_ALLOW_BLOCKED=1` is only for validating the harness in a restricted development environment and continues to report `BLOCKED`.
- CI installs the test-only Playwright dependency and Chromium before running E2E. Runtime manifest permissions, storage key and schema v2 are unchanged.
- No anti-bot bypass, Worker patch, or fabricated hardware/network/timing value was introduced.

### Verification boundary
- Unit/integration suite and build checks are run locally for this release. The local managed Chromium blocks extension loading; the live E2E result is therefore `BLOCKED`, not `PASS`. Run the same E2E command in an unmanaged Playwright Chromium environment and manually verify Kiwi/Quetta.

## 1.3.0 — document coverage and safer first run — 2026-10-12

### Confirmed behaviour fixes
- DNR profile rules now declare `condition.resourceTypes` explicitly. Target rules include `main_frame` and `sub_frame`; initiator-only rules remain limited to subresources. Regression tests inspect rule shape, including main document coverage.
- Dynamic MAIN-world registrations include `matchOriginAsFallback` only when Chromium is known to be 119+; unknown majors probe it. If the browser rejects the option, the registration is retried without it and Diagnostics reports `frame-limited` rather than promising opaque-frame coverage.
- Worker contexts are explicitly reported as `native-uncontrolled`. No `Worker`/`SharedWorker` constructor patch was added: CSP, module-worker resolution and site compatibility risks outweigh the incomplete coverage such a wrapper would provide.

### Defaults, compatibility and apply efficiency
- Fresh install and Reset now start `enabled: false`, recommending the closest bundled profile only when engine family/OS are recognized. Existing stored settings are preserved, including older partial records that historically defaulted to enabled.
- Storage key `veilProfileStudioSettingsV1`, schema v2, all 20 profile IDs and export envelope remain unchanged. `preferredMode` is additive within the schema-v2 rotation object and older values are normalized without a schema bump.
- Applying a rotation interval/preference change skips DNR rewrites, script replacement and tab injection when profile-applying settings did not change. The quick rotation toggle preserves a chosen Sequential mode.
- Preserves `Accept-CH` so sites can continue using native/compatible Client Hints. Continues to remove known high-entropy UA-CH request headers and strips `Critical-CH` response headers to avoid mandatory retries for removed hints.

### Validation
- `npm run check` and `npm test` pass; source tests cover explicit DNR resource types, the Chromium 119 feature threshold, rejected fallback registration, first-run/reset disabled behaviour, existing setting preservation, diff-only apply, Sequential mode persistence and offline self-test privacy.
- `npm run build` regenerates all 20 self-contained profile scripts and runs source/manifest/reference checks. Live Chromium E2E was not completed: managed policy in this environment blocks the unpacked-extension service worker from starting.

## 1.2.1 — Native machine-signal continuity — 2026-10-12

### Privacy consistency and bug fixes
- Removed synthetic overrides for `navigator.hardwareConcurrency`, `navigator.deviceMemory` and `navigator.maxTouchPoints`; the native browser/host values remain unchanged.
- `getHighEntropyValues()` keeps native `architecture`, `bitness`, `platformVersion`, `model`, `formFactors` and `wow64` values. Only browser-brand/version hints are aligned to the selected profile.
- Automatic rotation candidates are constrained to the detected native OS when the host exposes enough information; selecting a different OS still produces an explicit compatibility warning.
- Added source guardrails and build-time VM assertions against reintroducing synthetic hardware fields or changing timing/network/viewport surfaces.
- Updated tests to model Android explicitly for Android-rotation cases rather than relying on the Node runner's Linux navigator; added native OS classification and signal-policy regression coverage.

### Verification
- `npm run check` passes for 24 JavaScript files and 2 manifests.
- `npm test` passes 62/62 unit and integration tests.
- The build executes all 20 generated MAIN-world profiles in a VM, verifies native hardware/time/network/viewport values remain intact, and validates the three ZIP archives and checksums.

### Intentional limitation
- CPU/GPU performance, network RTT/bandwidth, clock/timezone, viewport, graphics and physical hardware are not fabricated or normalized. They remain measurable and may still identify the real environment; this is a consistency/privacy decision, not an anti-detection guarantee.

## 1.2.0 — Quick randomization and automatic rotation — 2026-10-12

### Features
- Added a popup **Random profile** action. It chooses from an existing configured pool when one has been selected, otherwise it prefers profiles with the same OS, architecture, form factor and runtime engine major when at least two exist. If that cohort is too small, it stays within the same device class and the UI continues to warn about profile/engine mismatch.
- Added popup controls for automatic random rotation and a quick interval selector (5, 10, 15, 30, 60, 120, or 240 minutes). The persisted validator supports 1–1440 minutes for workspace/imported settings.
- Implemented matching quick actions in the MV2 Legacy popup; MV2 remains headers-only and is not compatible with standard Chromium 139+.
- Uses Web Crypto for random selection when available, falling back to `Math.random()` only on hosts without Web Crypto. This is a profile-selection convenience, not a security or anti-detection boundary.

### Reliability and fixes
- Restores the named rotation alarm when the MV3 service worker starts and detects the alarm was dropped.
- Timed rotation remains opt-in. If alarms are unavailable or `alarms.create()` fails, configuration changes roll back and the UI does not falsely claim the schedule is active.
- Fixed popup disabled-state handling so controls reflect whether profile processing is enabled, and guarded the interval event against an uninitialized settings state.
- Added regression coverage for candidate-pool compatibility, random selection, interval validation, timer on/off, periodic profile changes, scheduler failure rollback and startup alarm recovery.

### Verification
- Test count increased from 51 to 60; all pass locally.
- Both MV3 and MV2 build variants retain the same product version `1.2.0` in that release.
- Automatic rotation is still not a guarantee of anonymity or undetectability. Websites may observe actual engine, GPU, worker, IP and other signals, and frequent switching can look less consistent.

## 1.1.1 — Manifest load fix — 2026-10-12

### Critical activation fix
- Removed `default_locale: "en"` from the MV3 manifest. The project ships its own UI translation module and no `_locales/en/messages.json`; Chromium requires `default_locale` to be absent when the `_locales` directory is not packaged. This invalid manifest field could prevent MV3 from loading at all.
- Bumped package and both manifest versions to `1.1.1` according to the bug-fix version rule.
- Added source/build validation that rejects a `default_locale` unless the matching `_locales/<locale>/messages.json` exists and parses as JSON. Added a regression test for both source manifests and generated package resources.
- Clarified MV2 support limits: standard Chromium 139+ no longer runs MV2 extensions. The Legacy ZIP remains for genuinely MV2-capable older browsers; it cannot be repaired for current Chromium by patching JavaScript.

### Verification
- Automated test suite and complete build run before repackaging.
- Manifest resource references, relative module imports, ZIP integrity, and SHA-256 checksums are checked.
- A desktop integration smoke test passed on Chromium `144.0.7559.96`: the MV3 service-worker target started, the popup rendered with 20 profiles, and `GET_SETTINGS` returned `{ ok: true, profileCount: 20 }`. The test environment’s managed extension blocklist was temporarily removed only for this isolated test and then restored.
- This is a desktop test, not certification on Kiwi/Quetta Android. MV2 was not expected to activate on this Chromium generation because standard Chromium 139+ no longer supports it.

## 1.1.0 — Adaptive mobile/browser API support — 2026-10-12

This release follows the product's requested semantic-version policy. The earlier `5.x` headings below are historical engineering checkpoints from the pre-1.0 prototype lineage; the current packaged product version at that historical checkpoint was `1.3.0`.

### Adaptive runtime support
- Added per-API feature detection for DNR, persistent dynamic content-script registration, MAIN-world `scripting.executeScript`, navigation events, frame querying, alarms, and legacy blocking webRequest. The browser-family label is informational; the available APIs determine the strategy.
- Added `full`/document-start, `adaptive`/navigation fallback, `headers-only`, `page-only`, and `limited` states in the UI and compact local diagnostics. Unsupported APIs are not silently described as available.
- Lowered the declared MV3 minimum Chromium version to `102`, the supported baseline for MAIN-world scripting; navigation fallback covers some incomplete APIs in newer mobile forks but cannot repair a rejected manifest/service-worker runtime.
- Fixed engine-major detection for Chromium forks such as Quetta, so `Quetta/2.x Chrome/139...` reports the embedded Chromium major instead of the Quetta app major.
- Updated UI compatibility warnings so Chromium-based mobile forks are not incorrectly reported as a browser-brand mismatch when using a Chrome-family profile.

### Robustness and bug fixes
- Removed a duplicate `webNavigation.onCommitted` listener that could inject the selected profile twice per navigation on adaptive hosts.
- Added runtime classification when `scripting.executeScript` rejects MAIN-world execution; page strategy downgrades to unavailable and records a fixed code instead of claiming continued support.
- Made DNR runtime failures non-fatal to available page scripting. Veil tries to clear its own stale dynamic rule IDs and reports a fixed failure code; unrelated dynamic rule IDs are left untouched.
- Made absence of the optional alarms API harmless while rotation is off, and awaits alarm creation when rotation is actually configured. Storage-write errors are now inside the settings transaction so rollback is attempted even when persistence fails.
- Guarded runtime manifest lookup so partial browser API shims degrade to an unknown identity instead of crashing detection.
- Added integration tests for exact single-injection fallback, Quetta engine-major detection, DNR failure/cleanup, and missing optional alarms.

### Verification scope
- `npm run check`, automated tests, full build, generated-script VM checks, manifest checks, and ZIP integrity checks are run before packaging.
- No real-device test on Quetta/Kiwi is claimed. The adaptive navigation strategy is best effort and can run later than page scripts.

## 5.5.0 — Legacy-manager trust fix and deeper error-leak audit — 2026-10-11

### Critical security fix
- Stopped invoking functions found through legacy `Symbol.for(...)` page globals. A website could forge one of those keys and receive the build authorization token when the extension attempted a migration call.
- Legacy page state is now treated as untrusted: when a custom UA getter and a legacy manager marker coexist, Veil refuses to stack another spoof and requires a real navigation. It does **not** claim to restore the old realm in place.
- The current page-state manager is versioned as anchor v4 and frozen with a frozen anchor; the build token is never passed to a manager discovered from page-visible state. Existing v3 anchors from Veil 5.3/5.4 are detected and require a clean page realm. MAIN-world state remains inspectable and is not a security boundary.
- Strengthened the static guard so adding a safe error helper elsewhere in a file no longer exempts direct raw-error forwarding at a call site.
- Corrected the manual checklist count and updated security audit notes to describe migration and residual risks precisely.

### Verification
- Re-run source validation, all unit/integration tests, fuzz checks, generated-script VM validation, repeated test loops and ZIP/manifest integrity checks before packaging.
- These checks do not substitute for an installation/smoke test on Kiwi Browser `139.0.7339.0`.

## 5.4.0 — Privacy/error hardening and repeated regression checks — 2026-10-11

### Fixed findings
- Added migration handling for the fixed global manager from Veil 5.2 (`veil-profile-studio.page-state-manager.v3`) as well as the original UA Switcher v2 manager. Native descriptors are restored first; a fresh page realm is required before Veil applies a new profile so the legacy non-configurable marker cannot cause the old spoof to be captured as native.
- Reworked current profile-state ownership to avoid a named global symbol or global helper; state remains attached only to the active custom UA getter, and tests verify native descriptor restoration.
- Standardized public failure codes and UI error display. Unexpected browser/extension exceptions are not surfaced verbatim, and diagnostics avoid URLs, stack traces, domains and request history.
- Added static source guardrails against runtime console logging, network telemetry clients, history/session APIs, HTML-string DOM sinks, `eval` and dynamic `Function` construction.
- Removed unused `tabs` permission, restricted explicit host access to HTTP(S), and limited open-tab/frame application concurrency to bounded workers.
- Added deterministic fuzz coverage for malformed domain/settings inputs, concurrency tests, and build-time execution/restoration checks for all 20 generated profiles.

### Verification scope
- Automated source checks, unit/integration tests, fuzz cases and package checks are run locally. These do not replace a live Kiwi `139.0.7339.0` test.
- A profile is not full anti-fingerprinting: the browser engine, HTTP/TLS behavior, IP/DNS, GPU/canvas/audio, fonts, viewport, locale/time zone, workers, and other surfaces remain outside extension control.

## 5.2.0 — Self-contained MAIN-world injection — 2026-10-11

### Critical privacy and integrity fix
- Removed the two-file MAIN-world injection sequence (`core.js` followed by profile data). A page could attempt to replace the writable global helper in the gap and inspect/alter the profile call.
- Each registered profile is now a single self-contained IIFE containing the profile core and authorization token; no `__veilApplyProfile` helper is exposed on the page global object.
- Added build-time execution checks for every generated profile in a browser-like VM: the expected UA/UAData values must be applied, the generated script must parse as classic JavaScript, and the global helper must remain absent.
- Made the 256-bit authorization token stable across Veil releases so already-open documents can continue switching/restoring profiles after an extension update. A known previous `ua-switcher.page-state-manager.v2` manager is asked to restore its native descriptors before Veil captures a new baseline.

### Revalidation
- Source validation, unit/integration tests, and build completed successfully after the change. Real Kiwi browser smoke testing remains outstanding.


## 5.1.0 — Standalone project / critical recovery fixes — 2026-10-11

### Project independence and UI
- Rebranded the extension to **Veil Profile Studio** with new shield icons, an original dark teal/violet workspace, refreshed popup, dedicated options page and a new local-only product namespace.
- Build output, settings export filenames, extension titles, diagnostics and CI artifact names use the new product identity. No upstream icon files or remote runtime code are included.
- Kept explicit MIT attribution for code lineage; “standalone” refers to its own runtime/UI/build, not a claim that all historical code was authored from scratch.

### Critical fixes
- Made MV3 content-script replacement rollback cover failures during both unregister and register. Recovery removes any partially active Veil scripts and restores the previous known registrations; if recovery also fails, an aggregate error is surfaced to diagnostics.
- Added an automated regression case for a partial unregister failure and verified that settings, DNR rules and registered scripts recover to the last known-good state.
- Replaced the universal hard-coded page token in tracked core source with a build placeholder. At 5.1.0 the builder generated a per-build token; 5.2.0 keeps a stable compatibility token so page manager state survives upgrades. Tokens are defense-in-depth, not cryptographic protection against someone who can inspect the installed package.
- Fixed the session-rotation startup path to use the same persist/apply/rollback path as manual settings changes.
- Removed the MV2 static `world: MAIN` content-script declaration and its `postMessage` bridge. MV2 now has an explicit **network-header-only** capability label rather than claiming JS spoofing that its manifest model cannot reliably provide.
- Removed unused authentication bridge code and tests, reducing attack surface and dead-code maintenance.
- Added tests preventing accidental reintroduction of the unsupported MV2 page bridge.

### Boundaries
- MV3 remains the complete build for modern Chromium. MV2 is a legacy request-header adapter and does not attempt to spoof `navigator` APIs.
- Browser-level tests on Kiwi `139.0.7339.0` have not been run in this build environment. Automated mock tests and packaging checks do not prove real-world compatibility.

## 5.0.0 — Standalone project identity
- Introduced the Veil Profile Studio namespace, shared profile core and new UI direction.

## 4.7.0 — Privacy consistency baseline
- Aligned common UA/Client Hints values; added domain-specific profiles, profile diagnostics and recovery behavior. See version history in the prior GĐ3 source package.

# Fingerprint-surface consistency audit — v3.1.0

**Purpose:** Help an operator assess consistency gaps on a website they control or are authorized to test. This is a local diagnostic report, not a promise of anonymity, a browser compatibility guarantee, or a mechanism for bypassing access controls.

## Coverage in the local self-test

| Surface | Status | What is checked | What remains unverified or unchanged |
|---|---|---|---|
| Main document UA / UA-CH | **Covered** | Selected profile versus exposed page values (`navigator.userAgent`, `navigator.userAgentData`). | Does not alter low-level TLS/TCP signatures or JA3 fingerprints. |
| Dedicated Worker | **Covered** | Prelude injection provides consistent identity on supported same-origin and blob scripts. | Cross-origin workers subject to strict CSP fall back to native. |
| SharedWorker | **Covered** | Prelude injection provides consistent identity where supported. | Same limitations as Dedicated Worker. |
| Audio Analyser / AudioBuffer | **Covered** | Idempotent deterministic jitter via `AudioBuffer.prototype.getChannelData` WeakMap cache. | Native audio latency and hardware sampling rate remain native. |
| Canvas 2D & OffscreenCanvas | **Covered** | Bounded multi-pixel deterministic perturbation skipping transparent pixels. | Deep WebGL shader compilation and timing remain host-native. |
| WebGL Rendering Context | **Covered** | Unmasked vendor and renderer masked when `WEBGL_debug_renderer_info` requested. | Native driver bugs, precision formats, and hardware limits remain native. |
| WebGPU | **Uncovered** | Reports native `navigator.gpu` adapter and limits. | Not spoofed in this build. |
| Geometry / TextMetrics | **Uncovered** | Native font rendering and bounding rects. | Not spoofed to avoid breaking document layouts. |
| Hardware Concurrency & Memory | **Covered** | Clamped to standard values (`cores`, `deviceMemory <= 8`). | Deep timing attacks can infer physical thread count. |
| Screen / Viewport | **Covered** | Clean `Screen.prototype` property spoofing without forcing `window.innerWidth/innerHeight`. | Native OS window chrome and physical display limits remain visible. |
| Media Queries | **Covered** | Input feature alignment (`pointer`, `hover`) with robust compound query evaluation. | Full CSS media query engine remains native browser implementation. |
| Touch Points | **Covered** | `navigator.maxTouchPoints` coherent with mobile/desktop configuration. | Touch event generation requires touchscreen hardware or DevTools emulation. |
| Plugins & MimeTypes | **Covered** | Coherent PDF viewer plugin array on desktop; empty on mobile. | Internal slot integrity preserved. |
| Geolocation | **Covered** | Virtual coordinates returned with full prototype inheritance without native prompt. | Accuracy circles and altitude variations not physically simulated. |
| Timezone & Locale | **Covered** | Timezone offset and Intl constructors synchronized via `formatToParts + Date.UTC`. | System clock skew and NTP synchronization remain host-native. |
| WebRTC IP Leak Shield | **Covered** | Host candidate filtering and browser-wide `default_public_interface_only` policy. | Peer-to-peer TURN/STUN relays remain functional. |
| Network Connection | **Uncovered** | `navigator.connection` reflects actual physical network state. | RTT, downlink, and effectiveType are not synthesized. |
