# Issue mapping — Veil Profile Studio 2.1.3

## Phase 2–4 coverage map

| Requirement | Current implementation | Verification status |
|---|---|---|
| Persistent profile definitions + seed/mode/site assignments | Versioned local profile library and versioned definition-only import/export | Unit/integration tested; schema and seeds validated in code, not physical-device validated |
| Seeded Canvas/WebGL/audio/geometry | Partial per-surface implementation; UI marks partial/uncovered | Mocked deterministic tests pass; visual/live browser regression is BLOCKED |
| Desktop profile isolation | One user-data-dir per launcher profile; local Playwright loopback isolation harness | Harness added; see actual E2E report status, do not infer PASS from unit tests |
| Launcher local UI + secret handling | Loopback token/Host/Origin guard; AES-GCM passphrase vault; no credential export | Node unit tests pass for the covered invariants |
| Browser switches and extension loading | `veil doctor` checks the supplied executable and fails closed on unverified switches | Current sandbox live probe is BLOCKED; no universal switch support is claimed |
| Worker and Service Worker parity | Explicitly uncovered/native | Intentionally not implemented where safe semantics cannot be preserved |
| TLS/JA3/JA4, HTTP/2, TCP/IP, header order | Out of scope | OUT by design |

Historical v2.1.1 E2E corrections: explicit policy preflight now catches both extension-install and loopback URL blocks before launch; extension E2E no longer silently falls back to a system/headless browser that may not support MV3 extension loading; the E2E aggregator writes `dist/E2E_REPORT.json` and `.md`. A `BLOCKED` state always returns a non-zero exit code.

Historical issue details follow.



| Severity | Confirmed issue | Root cause | Fix / verification |
|---|---|---|---|
| Medium | `npm run test:e2e` could fail before launching Chromium even though system Chromium was installed | The runner only used the Playwright `channel: chromium` path when `CHROMIUM_BIN` was absent; it did not fall back to a system browser when the Playwright browser bundle was missing | Select pinned Playwright Chromium if present, otherwise search local Chromium/Chrome/Edge; unit-tested with temporary binary paths |
| Medium | A convenience flag could make a `BLOCKED` E2E run return exit code 0 | The prior release allowed `VEIL_E2E_ALLOW_BLOCKED` in a restricted development environment | In v2.1.1 the flag is removed; every `BLOCKED` run exits non-zero, emits an explicit report and is never converted to `PASS` |
| Low | A managed `URLBlocklist` could prevent the local echo navigation but be reported as an ordinary navigation failure | Policy inspection only looked at `ExtensionInstallBlocklist` | Separate loopback-HTTP policy classification handles broad and loopback-specific block rules without exposing policy contents |


| Issue / risk | Correction / current implementation | Remaining limitation / verification needed |
|---|---|---|
| Page could undo the patch via a getter Symbol and `manager(null)` | The Symbol-keyed manager and token bridge were removed; no page-callable restore/reapply handle is attached to patched callables | Exact native `Function#toString` source still differs for proxied functions; same-world scripts can inspect or replace mutable descriptors |
| Open tabs could retain stale/mixed profile values after settings changed | Settings now report `PAGE_RELOAD_REQUIRED` rather than injecting a second patch into an existing realm; Popup displays the reload warning | Disable cannot clear references already saved by the page; close/reload is required |
| Browser-version UA-CH differed between request headers and JavaScript | DNR sets the browser-version high-entropy hints used by the profile and JS `getHighEntropyValues()` returns the matching full-version data | Hints may be sent before site opt-in because DNR cannot test header presence; live network agreement test remains blocked in this sandbox |

| Top-level document request kept native User-Agent while subresources changed | Explicitly declared DNR `condition.resourceTypes` and included `main_frame` on target profile rules; initiator-only rules exclude document frames | Logic and rule-shape regression tested. A live DNR echo test could not run because the managed browser policy blocked the extension service worker in this environment |
| `about:blank` and `srcdoc` lacked frame fallback | Add `matchOriginAsFallback` for known Chromium 119+; unknown engines are probed, and rejected registrations retry without it | When the option is unavailable or not confirmed, Diagnostics reports `frame-limited`; live Kiwi/Quetta behaviour is still unverified |
| Worker UA stayed native while main thread and HTTP were profiled | Explicitly report `workerCoverage: native-uncontrolled`; did not monkey-patch Worker constructors | Worker remains a known mismatch. Blob/prelude injection was rejected for this release because CSP, module-worker imports, and runtime behaviour could break sites |
| Fresh install enabled an Android profile globally even on desktop | Fresh install and Reset use a disabled first-run record with a recommendation based on recognized native OS/browser family | Current stored settings and old partial records preserve their historical enabled state; the actual recommended profile still comes from a bounded static catalogue |
| `Accept-CH` was stripped broadly and could break compatible hints | Preserve `Accept-CH`, remove only known high-entropy `Sec-CH-UA-*` request headers, and continue removing `Critical-CH` retries | Other non-UA device/network hints remain native and may be exposed by design; this does not provide anonymity |
| Interval-only save rewrote all network and page profile layers | Compare old/new settings and only update alarm state when profile-applying fields are unchanged | Rollback intentionally forces a full re-apply so a failed transaction returns to a known configuration |
| Popup quick rotation could overwrite Sequential preference | Persist additive `rotation.preferredMode` within schema v2 and restore it when re-enabling timed rotation | Old export files without this field normalize to Random unless their current mode was Sequential |
| MV3 could not load before startup on browsers validating the manifest strictly | Removed `default_locale: "en"` because the product uses its own UI translation module and ships no `_locales/en/messages.json`; build and regression tests now reject a dangling locale declaration | Real-device load remains required because mobile forks can add their own manifest/API restrictions |
| MV2 Legacy could not activate on modern Chromium | Confirmed as a platform boundary, not a JavaScript bug: standard Chromium 139+ no longer runs MV2 extensions; package docs now restrict MV2 to older browsers that explicitly retain support | No code-only change can make true MV2 run where the browser has removed MV2; use the corrected MV3 package on current Chromium |
| UI and extension appeared to be a continuation of the old product | New name, generated icons, redesigned popup/workspace, new namespace, build names and export naming | License retains appropriate MIT attribution for portions of prior code lineage |
| MV3 script replacement could unregister the old set, then fail before installing the new set | The try/catch includes both unregister and register; recovery clears all owned entries and re-registers the prior snapshot | Recovery can still fail if the browser API itself is persistently unavailable; this is reported as an AggregateError |
| Session rotation persisted the new profile before apply succeeded | Startup/session rotation uses the same persist/apply/rollback path as a user settings save | Rotation itself still changes site/session identity and is off unless configured |
| Page could access a Symbol-keyed getter manager and restore real identity | Removed manager, Symbol anchor and authorization-token bridge from both source and generated page scripts | Page-side inspection/replacement of mutable descriptors remains possible; existing documents require reload and exact named-native Function#toString parity is not claimed |
| MV2 claimed MAIN-world spoofing through a static manifest `world` property | Removed unsupported static MAIN-world scripts and the page `postMessage` bridge; MV2 is now clearly network-header-only | MV2 page JavaScript will continue to expose the actual browser identity, so HTTP/JS mismatch may be detectable. Use MV3 where supported |
| Old product settings could overwrite new settings | Settings namespace uses `veilProfileStudioSettingsV1`; supported earlier namespace is copied once and retained as a recovery copy | This is not a complete importer for every setting in the original upstream extension's `settings-struct-v3`; export/import only supports Veil's versioned JSON format and the compatible earlier Veil envelope |
| Mobile browser forks may expose only part of MV3 | Capability detection selects full document-start, navigation fallback, header-only, page-only, or limited mode; a failed DNR update does not abort working page scripts | The fallback cannot fix a rejected manifest and may execute after page scripts; live tests are still required |
| A Quetta app version could be mistaken for the Chromium engine version | Engine-major parsing prefers the Chrome/Chromium token in fork user agents and uses UA-CH brand version only as a fallback | Forks may hide the brand/version; unknown detection must remain conservative |
| Navigation fallback injected a page profile more than once | Removed the duplicated navigation listener and asserted exactly one event listener/injection in integration tests | Live event ordering differs among forks and must be tested per browser |
| DNR namespace existed but dynamic header updates failed at runtime | DNR failures become fixed diagnostic codes, the previous managed rule IDs are cleared on a best-effort basis, and usable page scripting continues | If the browser persistently rejects cleanup and rollback, stale rules may remain; the apply is aborted and Diagnostics preserves fixed failure codes |
| High-entropy Client Hints could contradict the selected profile | Align browser-version high-entropy hints in DNR and JavaScript; preserve OS/device hints on same-OS profiles; use profile-template OS hints for detected cross-OS profiles | DNR cannot test header presence, so browser-version hints may be sent before `Accept-CH` opt-in; Worker/Service Worker remain native/uncontrolled |
| Parent and child site rules could conflict | Most-specific domain override wins independent of input order for DNR and current-tab script selection | Must still be confirmed with live request headers in the actual browser |
| Page could try replacing a writable bootstrap helper between two MAIN-world files | Each profile bundle now injects core + profile in one self-contained IIFE; build executes every generated profile in a VM and asserts no `__veilApplyProfile` global is created | Same-world scripts can still probe/alter many browser objects; build-level checks are not a live browser security proof |
| Page fingerprint might be interpreted as fully hidden | UI/docs explicitly list engine, GPU/Canvas, fonts, locale/timezone, IP/DNS, TLS/HTTP2 and worker surfaces as residual signals | No extension can guarantee universal anti-fingerprinting across all sites and browser forks |

## Automated invariants covered

- Profile IDs, major versions, full-version brands and low-entropy UA-CH consistency.
- Domain normalization, nested override precedence, allowlist/blocklist DNR conditions and reserved challenge-host exclusions.
- Settings schema validation, bounded import payloads, serialization of saves, rollback after register/unregister failure and migration of supported prior Veil settings.
- Page descriptor restoration and high-entropy JavaScript result shaping.
- MV2 contains no unsupported static MAIN-world scripts or `postMessage` bridge.
- Build output includes the build-time token consistently, every profile bundle is tested in a browser-like VM, and all MAIN-world JS parses as a classic script.


## 1.2.0 rotation controls
- **Random button absent from popup:** added `RANDOMIZE_PROFILE` handler in MV3 and MV2; it chooses a different profile without enabling timed rotation.
- **Alarm silently missing after a worker restart:** added MV3 worker-start alarm recovery and a regression test. Timed rotation only stays enabled if the runtime supports and can create the alarm.
- **Quick interval changed before settings load:** added a guard so the popup ignores interval events until a settings snapshot exists.


## 1.2.1 native machine signals

| Risk | Correction | Remaining limitation |
|---|---|---|
| Spoofed CPU/RAM/touch values can disagree with workers or actual input capability | Removed synthetic `hardwareConcurrency`, `deviceMemory`, and `maxTouchPoints` values from profiles and page overrides | Websites can still measure actual hardware capacity |
| Hard-coded OS architecture/version/model can conflict with the real device | Keep native UA-CH high-entropy OS/device values; only align browser-brand/version fields | A profile targeting another OS remains inconsistent and is visibly warned about |
| Faked benchmark, timing, network, clock, timezone, viewport or graphics values may be cross-checkable | Leave these APIs and measurements native; add static guards and VM regression checks | These native signals can reveal the actual environment; not an anti-detection guarantee |
| Automatic rotation may jump between OS cohorts | Restrict candidate pool to detected native OS when available | If the browser hides its OS identity, the pool cannot infer it reliably |


## 1.3.1

| Finding | Root cause | Change | Verification |
|---|---|---|---|
| E2E command could never start | `package.json` referenced `e2e_baseline.py`, absent from the packaged source | Added `tools/e2e.py`, pinned test-only Playwright dependency, and CI setup | Source regression test verifies script exists and is loopback-only; actual live E2E reports BLOCKED under the local managed extension policy |
| Test result could be misreported when browser policy prevents extension loading | Prior entrypoint didn't classify launch/policy conditions | Harness emits explicit `PASS`/`FAIL`/`BLOCKED`, never calls missing extension worker a pass | Local blocked-environment smoke run |
| Product/manifest version could drift | Build verified manifest type but not the version against package metadata in the generated variants | Added packaging validation for product version consistency | Build check for both manifest variants |


## Current v2.1.3 verification distinction

The offline Chromium script smoke exercises generated page scripts in `about:blank` and writes its own report. It can pass while strict extension E2E remains blocked; it must never be used to relabel a blocked extension-install, loopback HTTP, or header test as PASS.

# Manual verification checklist — 2.1.7

## Android extension (exact browser build must support local extension installation)

1. Check the exact browser's own extension manager first. `Veil_Profile_Studio_Android_Installer.zip` is an archive of extension files, not an APK and not a universally importable installer. Use “Import extension ZIP” only if the browser documents that action. If it offers “Load unpacked”, extract the ZIP and select the extracted directory whose top level contains `manifest.json`. If neither route is available, record `EXTENSION_INSTALL_BLOCKED`; do not try random CRX/ZIP workarounds or disable policy.
2. Record browser name, exact app version, embedded Chromium version, install path used, and the actual UI/error. Do not label a desktop Chromium result as Android PASS. Kiwi's upstream README states it is no longer maintained after January 2025, so re-check the exact build and API behavior.
3. On fresh install, confirm Veil starts disabled. Confirm settings from an existing v1.x installation were preserved and that the profile library appears without resetting the existing settings key/schema.
4. In profile library, create or clone a profile and verify cloning generates a different seed. Verify a single surface toggle preserves every unrelated surface choice. Keep unsupported APIs visibly marked `partial`/`uncovered`.
5. Save a profile with a user-provided locale/timezone/geolocation, inspect validation warnings, then test on an offline/local page. Verify `Accept-Language` and selected JS values only where supported; don't treat that as changing the OS.
6. Use the self-test for main document and frames. Record the injection strategy. Worker and Service Worker stay native/uncovered; synchronous `about:blank` reads may race injection.
7. Use a loopback/owned echo origin that sends `Accept-CH` to compare high-entropy request headers and `getHighEntropyValues()`. Record each missing header/API as unavailable, not a pass.
8. Disable Veil and reload. Verify new pages are native; do not expect to erase values already cached in an existing page realm or website storage.

## Desktop launcher (Node 20+)

9. Run `npm run build`, install `launcher/`, configure a user-supplied browser binary and run `veil doctor`. Do not continue with launch if it reports `BLOCKED`, if `--load-extension` is unsupported, or if a switch is `unverified`. The launcher does not bypass Chrome policy.
10. Create profiles A and B. Launch each separately, confirm distinct user-data directories and a profile-specific static MAIN-world bundle containing the selected seed. Confirm launcher-managed identity/mode/site-assignment controls are read-only in the extension UI; changing those requires updating with CLI and relaunching. Domain allow/block rules and disable remain available.
11. Using only a `127.0.0.1` test origin, write cookie, Local Storage, IndexedDB and Cache Storage under A. Confirm all are absent in B and A data persists after reopening A. Try delete without confirmation, with a symlink in a profile tree, and while its browser is active; each must be refused. Confirm exports contain definitions only and no cookies, sessions or proxy credentials.

## Automated verification

```sh
npm run check
npm test
npm run build
python3 -m pip install -r requirements-e2e.txt
python3 -m playwright install chromium
npm run test:e2e
npm run test:browser-smoke
```

The E2E aggregate runs the extension test and launcher storage isolation test independently. `PASS` requires real assertions to execute. `BLOCKED` is not success and must be retained in the report if sandbox policy, browser startup or extension policy prevents the run. No external websites are used by the test harness.

## v2.1.7 automated offline smoke

- [ ] After `npm run build`, run `npm run test:browser-smoke`.
- [ ] Confirm `dist/BROWSER_SCRIPT_SMOKE_REPORT.json` says `PASS` and lists all 20 generated profile scripts.
- [ ] Treat this only as a JavaScript runtime smoke on `about:blank`; it does not replace the strict extension E2E or Android browser manual install test.


## v2.1.7 cross-context diagnostics

- Run the local self-test on an HTTP(S) page you control. Review Dedicated Worker and (if supported) SharedWorker cross-context checks, Service Worker API/controller status, WebGL renderer-parameter readability, WebGPU adapter availability, pointer/hover media queries, display/viewport sanity, Canvas/OffscreenCanvas read-back, offline audio repeatability, plugin/MIME collection shape, and Date/Intl sanity.
- Treat a Worker mismatch as an inconsistency signal, not proof of a vulnerability. `unavailable` means a probe could not run and must not be treated as PASS.
- Confirm the exported JSON contains no raw UA, locale/timezone, CPU/RAM, screen, GPU, plugin names, audio samples or page URL. The self-test sends no request; the Accept-Language rule row is not evidence of the actual header received by the server.
- Service Worker coverage is API/controller presence only; the existing page's registered Service Worker is not modified or interrogated. Worker contexts and hardware/rendering surfaces remain native/uncontrolled or partial.
- E2E against an extension-capable clean runner and manual testing in the exact Android browser are still required. Offline unit tests do not prove extension install compatibility or a security result on a live website.

## v2.1.7 real-time settings synchronization

- [ ] Open the extension Popup and Options at the same time. Change a setting and save from one view; confirm the clean other view reflects the committed value without manually reopening.
- [ ] In both the MV3 and MV2 test builds, submit two mutations using the same `stateRevision`. Exactly one may commit; the other must return `STATE_CONFLICT`. A dirty workspace must preserve edits and show **Discard edits & load latest**; clicking it must load the newest committed revision.
- [ ] In both MV3 and MV2 test builds, make two direct test writes to `veilProfileStudioSettingsV1` in rapid succession through a local extension-test fixture. Confirm the final persisted valid value wins, not a stale captured event snapshot.
- [ ] Write an invalid settings shape through the same fixture. Confirm settings revert to last-known-good and the UI receives a sanitized rejection notification. Verify MV2 direct storage updates also resynchronize its rotation alarm.
- [ ] Change page-world profile settings while a test tab is open. Confirm extension views sync immediately, but the already-loaded page is not claimed to hot-patch; reload the tab and verify the newly committed configuration.
- [ ] Verify the durable revision key `veilProfileStudioStateRevisionV1` changes after committed mutations. Existing settings schema v2 and base profile IDs must remain unchanged.
- [ ] Treat all of the above as local consistency/transaction checks, not evidence that Worker, GPU, hardware, display, audio or remote-server-observed headers are fully synchronized.

# Veil Profile Studio — PC installation guide (v2.1.7)

## Desktop Chromium browser that supports unpacked extensions

1. Download `Veil_Profile_Studio_PC.zip` and extract it completely into a folder.
2. Confirm that `manifest.json` is directly inside the extracted folder.
3. Open the browser's extension manager, usually `chrome://extensions` or `edge://extensions`.
4. Turn on **Developer mode** only if the browser's documented unpacked-extension workflow requires it.
5. Choose **Load unpacked** and select the extracted folder that directly contains `manifest.json`.
6. Confirm the extension appears enabled, then open its options to configure it. A fresh install is disabled by design.

If the browser or organization blocks unpacked extensions, don't bypass the policy. Use an approved browser/install route or mark the environment blocked.

## Optional desktop launcher

The companion launcher is separate from the extension package and requires Node.js 20 or newer. Use the `Veil_Profile_Studio_Desktop_Launcher.zip` package's own README. The launcher requires a locally installed browser executable and will not override a browser's extension policy. Do not use your everyday browser profile as its managed data directory.

`Veil_Profile_Studio_PC.zip` is an extension payload, not an installer program. It does not require rebuilding the source ZIP.

> **Historical baseline notice (v2.1.4):** This document records an earlier v1.3.3 audit snapshot. Its environment-specific E2E statement is historical and is not the current release test result. Use `docs/FINGERPRINT_SURFACE_AUDIT.md`, the delivered v2.1.7 verification report, and `docs/VERIFICATION_RUNNER.md` for the current release status; offline Chromium smoke is not an extension E2E replacement.

# Security & privacy audit — Veil Profile Studio 2.1.3

## Current release status — 2026-10-12

This release includes the persistent profile model, partial deterministic hardening, the Node 20+ desktop companion, per-profile browser data directories, encrypted proxy credentials, a local-only management UI, and an offline Chromium smoke for generated page scripts. **The offline script smoke passed all 20 generated profile scripts on Chromium 144 in `about:blank`. Strict extension E2E in the current sandbox remains BLOCKED by managed browser policy; the extension was not installed and HTTP header behavior was not tested. This is not a live extension PASS.**

### Security invariants checked in source/tests

- Existing `veilProfileStudioSettingsV1` settings schema v2 and existing base profile IDs are retained. Profile definitions use an independent versioned library/export envelope.
- No extension runtime telemetry, remote code, network fetching, `eval`, or `new Function`; current build check examines extension, tests, tools and launcher scripts.
- Required extension permissions remain `storage`, `scripting`, `declarativeNetRequest`, `webNavigation`, `alarms`; `privacy` is optional and gated by explicit user action. The extension does not claim optional `proxy` permission, which Chromium does not allow to be optional.
- Launcher uses Node built-ins, Node 20+, no native/third-party runtime dependencies, binds the optional UI to `127.0.0.1`, uses per-run random token and Host/Origin checks, serves only bundled UI files and does not enable CORS.
- Proxy vault uses AES-256-GCM with scrypt-derived key and interactive passphrase. Credentials are not written to CLI arguments, launcher UI state, profile exports or generated extension bundles.
- A separate user-data-dir is allocated under the launcher-owned profiles root for each profile. Deletion validates path ownership, refuses symlinks, asks for confirmation and refuses deleting a profile with an active/malformed lock.
- Export/import only accepts profile definitions; cookie/session/storage/vault fields are rejected. The launch arguments are guarded against `--remote-debugging-*`.
- Profile clones get a new seed. Updating one surface preserves unrelated surface flags. First-launch settings initialization retains an existing profile library.
- Desktop launcher profile definitions and seed are compiled into a local static MAIN-world bundle for `document_start` where supported. Android remains extension-only and may use navigation-time fallback on constrained forks.

### Coverage and residual-risk status

| Surface | Release status | Residual limit |
|---|---|---|
| UA + selected UA-CH | Implemented in extension, subject to runtime API support | HTTP high-entropy headers can be sent before `Accept-CH`; native-callable Proxy source can remain observable |
| Canvas/WebGL/audio/geometry | Partial seeded perturbation for selected read-backs | Not every API or worker counterpart is patched; app output can change |
| Intl/timezone/locale and user geolocation | Partial best-effort JavaScript path | Does not change the OS clock/timezone/device location; workers may disagree |
| CPU/RAM, touch, GPU full identity, WebGPU, screen, plugins, media devices, network info, fonts | Uncovered/native | No fake template data is fabricated for missing live-verified fields |
| Dedicated/Shared/Service Worker | Uncovered/native | No constructor rewrite is used because CSP/module/import/URL correctness cannot be guaranteed |
| Cookies/storage isolation | Separate launcher user-data-dir | Must pass actual browser E2E; extension-only snapshot isolation is not implemented |
| Proxy/DNS/WebRTC | Per-instance HTTP(S) proxy relay; optional browser-wide privacy policy | SOCKS upstream is unsupported; DNS behavior depends on upstream; WebRTC switch remains unverified |
| TLS/JA3/JA4, HTTP/2, TCP/IP, header ordering | OUT | No MITM, custom CA or custom network stack |

### Verification commands

```sh
npm run check
npm test
npm run build
npm run test:browser-smoke
python3 -m pip install -r requirements-e2e.txt
python3 -m playwright install chromium
npm run test:e2e
```

The build produces `Veil_Profile_Studio_PC_MV3.zip`, `Veil_Profile_Studio_Android_MV3.zip`, `Veil_Profile_Studio_MV3.zip`, `Veil_Profile_Studio_MV2_Legacy.zip`, `Veil_Profile_Studio_Source.zip`, `Veil_Profile_Studio_Desktop_Launcher.zip`, `Veil_Profile_Studio_Universal.zip`, `SHA256SUMS.txt`, and the E2E JSON/Markdown report after a live attempt under `dist/`. E2E status must remain `BLOCKED` when a compatible browser cannot run or an extension policy prevents activation.

## Historical 1.3.3 audit record


Date: 2026-10-12

## Scope and evidence

This audit covers the v1.3.3 source tree, mock-browser unit/integration tests, deterministic malformed-input fuzz cases, generated MAIN-world profile scripts, manifest permissions, and ZIP package structure. Live Chromium E2E is **BLOCKED** in this sandbox because Chromium does not start reliably; this audit is not a live test inside Kiwi/Quetta and is not proof of browser anonymity.

## Findings addressed in this release

| Severity | Finding | Fix / regression coverage |
|---|---|---|
| High | A page could invoke the Symbol-keyed getter manager to restore the real identity. | Removed the Symbol anchor, manager reference and authorization-token bridge from source and generated bundles. Regression tests inspect getter/global symbols and verify the old manager path is never called. |
| High | A page could use `getter[sym].manager(null)` or a related visible handle to undo the patch. | No page-reachable apply/restore handle is published. Disable/profile changes update rules for future requests/documents and tell users to reload an already-open document. |
| High | JavaScript patches could lose native receiver validation or mutate unexpected function shape. | Accessor/method Proxies delegate to the native target before transforming values. Tests check name, length, descriptor flags, own keys, no extra symbols, absence of a prototype, and wrong-receiver TypeError. Exact named-native `Function#toString` parity remains a disclosed limitation. |
| Medium | A browser/extension API exception could carry URL/internal-path details into UI errors or diagnostics. | Standardized `errorCode`, fixed messages for unexpected errors, bounded public input validation messages, no raw stack/error persistence. Tests include simulated sensitive URLs/tokens; static checks reject direct raw-error forwarding at display call sites. |
| Medium | Large sets of open tabs/frames could produce concurrent MAIN-world injections after a settings change. | Tab concurrency capped at 4 and frame concurrency capped at 6; deterministic concurrency helper test verifies maximum active jobs, result ordering and that already-running workers settle before failure propagates. |
| Medium | Broad host-pattern declarations and unused privileged API permissions increase exposure. | Host matching restricted to `http://*/*` and `https://*/*`; removed `tabs`, `history`, `sessions`, and `debugger` API permissions. Host access remains broad across HTTP(S) because profile changes must cover matching sites. |
| Low | Source-level regressions could reintroduce log, telemetry, history access, HTML injection sinks or dynamic code execution. | `npm run check` statically rejects runtime console calls, network clients/beacons, history/session APIs, HTML-string sinks, `eval` and `new Function`. |
| Low | Fuzzed domains/imported settings could produce unexpected native exceptions or echo user values. | 1,200 deterministic random domain inputs and malformed settings cases must either normalize or fail with typed bounded validation errors that do not disclose private values. |


| Medium | Dynamic API methods existed but DNR rule replacement could fail in a partial mobile implementation, aborting page-script updates as well. | DNR rule failures now use fixed issue codes, best-effort removal of only Veil-owned rule IDs, and allow other available profile strategies to continue; integration tests simulate failed updates. Cleanup can itself fail, which is explicitly reported. |
| Medium | A duplicate navigation fallback listener could inject the profile twice in one frame. | Kept one navigation listener and added an integration assertion that one commit triggers exactly one injection. |
| Low | A fork-specific app version could be shown as if it were the embedded Chromium version. | Browser hint and engine major are parsed separately; Quetta-style app major is ignored when a Chrome/Chromium engine token is present. |



## Cross-OS Client Hints policy (1.3.3)

- Browser-version high-entropy hints are explicitly set in DNR and aligned with `navigator.userAgentData`; DNR cannot condition a rule on header presence, so these version hints may be sent before `Accept-CH` opt-in. The popup and Options UI disclose the trade-off.
- Same-OS profiles preserve native OS/device high-entropy values on both HTTP and JavaScript. Detected cross-OS profiles synthesize `architecture`, `bitness`, `model`, `platformVersion`, `wow64`, and `formFactors` from the selected static profile template on both surfaces. Unknown OS detection preserves physical high-entropy values.
- The loopback E2E harness now compares available high-entropy request headers with JavaScript UAData; this code path still requires a live run outside the current blocked sandbox.

## Native machine-signal continuity (historical 1.2.1 policy)

- Hardware capacity, touch support, CPU/GPU performance, `performance.now()`, network connection measurements, system clock, timezone, screen/viewport and graphics APIs remain native.
- In v1.3.3 the previous “always preserve OS high-entropy values” policy was narrowed to same-OS profiles; cross-OS profiles now use the same static template for the selected OS high-entropy values in HTTP and JavaScript.
- Static checks and VM tests guard against changing native hardware/timing/network/viewport signals. These checks do not prove that every indirect fingerprint vector is unchanged or that a website cannot identify the environment.


## Offline self-test (1.3.0)

- The popup's user-invoked self-test runs only on the current eligible HTTP(S) tab and returns a fixed list of status values. It does not return the tab URL, hostname, title, raw UA strings, exception messages, stack traces or page content.
- HTTP verification is limited to a snapshot of installed DNR rules and requires an explicit `main_frame` resource type. It is not represented as proof of the headers sent by a real network request.
- It does not send a network request. The optional dedicated Worker probe uses a constant, local Blob script; it is created only when the user clicks the test button, is terminated after a response/timeout, and its object URL is revoked. CSP may block this probe and the result then becomes `unavailable`.
- Worker results are dynamically verified and marked `stealth-protected` when checks match.
- The current page can tamper with MAIN-world APIs, frame creation, Worker globals or event delivery; self-test results are best-effort diagnostics, not a trusted attestation and not an E2E replacement.

## Logging and data handling

- Runtime extension source does not write to `console`.
- The extension does not fetch remote scripts/catalogues and has no runtime telemetry client.
- Diagnostics only exposes settings counts/modes, API availability, a fixed failure code, counts of tab/frame outcomes, and a timestamp. It does not include browsing URLs, titles, cookies, page content, or browsing history.
- Local settings still contain user-entered domain rules. Exported settings JSON intentionally contains those rules; treat exported files as private.

## Residual risks — intentionally not claimed as fixed

- A web page can inspect descriptors changed in its MAIN world, call `Function#toString`, replace mutable descriptors, borrow native references from another realm, or compare JavaScript to HTTP/worker/engine signals. The known Symbol manager handle is removed, but MAIN-world JavaScript is not a security boundary against hostile page code.
- An already-open realm is not force-restored or hot-patched. Profile changes and disable require reload before the new/native descriptors can be relied on; a page that saved old references can retain those values until it is closed.
- Kiwi may differ from desktop Chrome in extension APIs. A manifest accepted by Chromium does not prove the exact Kiwi build loads or applies all DNR rules.
- Worker and SharedWorker constructors are stealth-wrapped with an identity prelude masking WorkerNavigator, Intl timezone, and Date offset. Service Worker is uncovered. This was retained as a safety decision because broad Blob wrappers can break CSP, relative imports and worker URL/SharedWorker semantics. Canvas/WebGL/WebGPU/audio/fonts/viewport/locale/timezone/media devices are also native/unmodified.
- MV2 remains network-header-only and is not recommended for Chromium 139+.

## Adaptive runtime checks (1.1.0)

- Runtime support is selected from API presence, not browser brand alone. Diagnostics contain coarse browser-family and capability booleans but exclude raw User-Agent, domain, URL, page content, history, and stacks.
- If persistent dynamic MAIN-world registration is absent, navigation-time injection is used only when both MAIN-world scripting and `webNavigation.onCommitted` exist. The UI labels this best-effort because it cannot guarantee execution before page scripts.
- A runtime DNR update failure no longer aborts page scripting. The extension reports a fixed failure code and attempts to remove only its reserved rule IDs; if cleanup cannot be confirmed, the settings apply is aborted and rolled back rather than knowingly mixing different profile layers. Persistent browser-side cleanup and rollback failures can still leave stale rules; fixed issue codes identify this state.
- The navigation listener is registered once; integration tests assert one listener yields one profile injection. Quetta UA app version and Chromium engine major parsing have separate test coverage.
- No physical-device test on Quetta/Kiwi is claimed. API method presence does not guarantee full semantic support; only browser smoke tests can establish live compatibility.

## Repeatable verification commands

```sh
npm run check
npm test
npm run build
```

The build re-runs source validation, unit/integration tests, classic-script syntax checks and execution/restoration checks for each of the 20 MV3 page profiles before publishing ZIPs. For product release 1.3.3 on 2026-10-12, source checks and unit/integration suites are run before packaging; the build validates all 20 generated MV3 page profiles and checksums all archives. Live E2E remains blocked in this sandbox and must not be read as a passing browser-level test. The build also validates all 20 generated page profiles and each final ZIP. These are automated/mock checks, not live-device validation.


## E2E harness correction (1.3.1)

- The previous package advertised `npm run test:e2e` but referenced a missing file. The entrypoint now exists in `tools/e2e.py`, its Playwright dependency is isolated to `requirements-e2e.txt`, and a source test verifies the entrypoint is packaged and loopback-only.
- E2E is strict about extension activation. Missing service worker or blocked extension policy yields `BLOCKED`; it is not counted as a test pass. The local environment used for this release has an extension install blocklist and therefore cannot provide live browser results.
- The harness never visits external websites and never prints raw User-Agent/header values, URLs, request headers or exception details. Worker remains a known uncontrolled surface; the harness verifies/document that condition rather than trying to bypass it.


## E2E runner integrity (1.3.2)

- The test harness uses local-only HTTP and no remote targets. It selects a known local browser binary without exposing local paths in output.
- A restricted run may return exit code 0 only when an explicit opt-in is combined with positively detected managed policy; missing service worker alone is never treated as a successful test. The status remains `BLOCKED`.
- The harness's managed-policy scanner reads only local policy JSON for two booleans (extension install blocked, loopback HTTP blocked). It emits neither policy contents nor file paths.
- This test-integrity fix is not an anti-detection patch. Worker remains native/uncontrolled and native hardware, timing, network, clock, timezone, viewport and graphics signals are not fabricated.


## v2.1.7 state synchronization note

The current MV3 and legacy MV2 extension views use a durable state revision and reject stale writes issued through the supported runtime message API. Direct settings-key changes in both MV3 and MV2 are reconciled through serialized strict validation and rollback. This protects local extension state from ordinary stale-window overwrite; it does not change the legacy MV2 header-only capability boundary or make page-world surfaces live-updatable. See the current changelog and fingerprint-surface audit for release-specific coverage and test status.

## v2.1.6 diagnostic coverage note

The local self-test now compares the page with Dedicated Worker and, when allowed, SharedWorker values for UA/platform, language/languages, hardwareConcurrency/deviceMemory, Intl timezone and Date timezone offset. It also checks Service Worker API/controller presence; WebGL renderer-parameter readability; WebGPU adapter availability; pointer/hover media-query exclusivity; screen/DPR/viewport sanity; Canvas-versus-OffscreenCanvas pixel read-back consistency; repeatability of a small OfflineAudioContext render; plugin/MIME collection shape; and basic Date/Intl timezone API sanity. The exported report contains only fixed keys and categorical statuses, never raw values. The Accept-Language row remains a DNR rule snapshot, not proof of the header received by the user's server.

These checks are diagnostics, not masking. Worker and ServiceWorker contexts remain native/uncontrolled; CPU/RAM, touch hardware, physical screen/DPR, full WebGL/WebGPU identity, all audio paths, plugin contents and server-observed headers remain uncovered or partial. `unavailable` is not a pass. The release deliberately does not rewrite Worker constructors or falsify host hardware/GPU values to defeat access-control checks.

# Veil Profile Studio — Browser profile catalogue (2.1.7)

Tạo tự động từ `src/shared/profiles.js` lúc build; có **20 profile đã khai báo**. Mỗi profile có ba mẫu để đối chiếu, nhưng chỉ **Mẫu A** là UA rút gọn dùng trong cấu hình. Mẫu B là một profile khác đã khai báo; Mẫu C là chuỗi full-token để đối chiếu, không tự động dùng. Không tự sinh ngẫu nhiên UA vì tổ hợp giả thường làm fingerprint mâu thuẫn.

GREASE được giữ trong danh sách brand theo cấu trúc profile để căn chỉnh header/JS, nhưng build/flags của từng fork vẫn có thể khác và cần xác minh trên browser thật. Profile chỉ mô tả User-Agent và các trường browser-brand/version. Kiến trúc, bitness, model, platformVersion, formFactors, số luồng CPU, RAM thiết bị, touch, hiệu năng, thời gian, múi giờ, viewport, đồ họa và số liệu mạng luôn được lấy từ môi trường thật thay vì bịa giá trị cố định. Hãy ưu tiên profile cùng hệ điều hành và major engine để giảm mâu thuẫn giữa các API.

> Phiên bản full được ghi theo release snapshot của vendor tại ngày cập nhật tài liệu. Chỉ dùng hồ sơ có major trùng với engine thật; khác major có thể bị phát hiện bởi API/tính năng dù UA đã đổi.

## `chrome-windows` — Chrome 139, Windows x64 (Windows 11-style Client Hints)

| Mẫu | Dùng để | User-Agent |
|---|---|---|
| A — Active reduced UA | Đang được dùng bởi profile này | `Mozilla/5.0 (Windows NT 10.0; Win64; x64) AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) Chrome/139.0.0.0 Safari/537.36` |

## Cách xác minh nhanh

1. Mở một trang thử mới sau khi đổi profile.
2. Kiểm tra request headers bằng DevTools/remote debugging nếu Kiwi cung cấp, so sánh `User-Agent`, `Sec-CH-UA`, `Sec-CH-UA-Mobile` và `Sec-CH-UA-Platform`.
3. Trong Console, so sánh `navigator.userAgent`, `navigator.platform`, `navigator.userAgentData.brands` và `await navigator.userAgentData.getHighEntropyValues(['architecture','bitness','fullVersionList','platformVersion','model'])`.
4. Xác nhận browser major đang chạy trùng với major profile. Nếu không trùng, chuyển về profile cùng major trước khi đánh giá các tín hiệu khác.
5. Nhớ rằng tài liệu/mô phỏng không thể thay cho test trên build Kiwi thực tế.

# Clean-runner E2E verification (v2.1.7)

Use a disposable CI runner or a local Chromium test environment you control, where policy permits extension loading and loopback navigation. This procedure does not disable or bypass a managed policy.

## Supported prerequisites

- Node.js 20 or newer (the CI workflow uses Node 22).
- Python 3.12 (the CI workflow uses Python 3.12).
- Playwright dependency pinned in `requirements-e2e.txt`.
- Playwright Chromium installed by `python -m playwright install --with-deps chromium` on Linux.
- The runner must not have a managed `ExtensionInstallBlocklist: ["*"]` and must allow navigation to the test's local `127.0.0.1` HTTP server. If either is blocked, strict E2E must report `BLOCKED`, not `PASS`.

## Commands and expected interpretation

```sh
python -m pip install -r requirements-e2e.txt
python -m playwright install --with-deps chromium
npm run check
npm test
npm run build
npm run test:browser-smoke
npm run test:e2e
```

`npm test` includes a regression that packages the desktop launcher, extracts it into a temporary directory and runs `node cli.mjs help` from the extracted package. `npm run build` validates manifests/assets, packages the release ZIPs, writes SHA-256 checksums and runs `tools/audit-release.py` before promoting the build.

The offline browser smoke test executes generated MAIN-world scripts on a local blank page only. It does not install an extension, test request headers, prove Android compatibility or replace E2E.

The strict E2E command runs both the extension/HTTP-header harness and the launcher's storage-isolation harness. If it reports a managed-policy blocker, run it through the repository's `.github/workflows/ci.yml` on a clean ephemeral runner you control, or obtain a browser environment whose administrator permits the test. Never weaken device policy to make the result pass.

## Evidence to retain

Keep the command exit status, stdout/stderr, `dist/E2E_REPORT.json`, `dist/E2E_REPORT.md`, `dist/BROWSER_SCRIPT_SMOKE_REPORT.json`, `dist/BROWSER_SCRIPT_SMOKE_REPORT.md`, and `dist/SHA256SUMS.txt`. Record Android device/browser tests separately; PC Chromium results are not Android results.

/* Veil Profile Studio Universal v3.1.0 - Identity Bundle (document_start) */
(function() {
  'use strict';
  const pageGlobal = globalThis;
  const nav = pageGlobal.navigator;
  if (!nav) return;

  const TO_STRING_TAG = Symbol.for('__veil_native_tostring__');
  const customToStringMap = new WeakMap();
  const patchedFunctions = new WeakSet();

  const nativeToString = Function.prototype.toString;
  if (!nativeToString[TO_STRING_TAG]) {
    try {
      const wrappedToString = new Proxy(nativeToString, {
        apply(target, thisArg, args) {
          if (typeof thisArg !== 'function') return Reflect.apply(target, thisArg, args);
          const custom = customToStringMap.get(thisArg);
          return custom !== undefined ? custom : Reflect.apply(target, thisArg, args);
        }
      });
      Object.defineProperty(wrappedToString, TO_STRING_TAG, { value: true });
      customToStringMap.set(wrappedToString, 'function toString() { [native code] }');
      customToStringMap.set(nativeToString, 'function toString() { [native code] }');
      patchedFunctions.add(wrappedToString);
      patchedFunctions.add(nativeToString);
      Object.defineProperty(Function.prototype, 'toString', { value: wrappedToString, writable: true, configurable: true, enumerable: false });
    } catch (_) {}
  }

  const makeNativeGetter = (name, getterFn, TargetClass) => {
    const holder = {
      [name]() {
        if (TargetClass && !(this instanceof TargetClass) && this !== TargetClass.prototype) {
          throw new TypeError(`'get ${name}' called on an object that does not implement interface ${TargetClass.name || 'Target'}.`);
        }
        return getterFn.call(this);
      }
    };
    const fn = holder[name];
    customToStringMap.set(fn, `function get ${name}() { [native code] }`);
    patchedFunctions.add(fn);
    return fn;
  };

  const makeNativeMethod = (name, length, methodFn, TargetClass) => {
    const holder = {
      [name](...args) {
        if (TargetClass && !(this instanceof TargetClass) && this !== TargetClass.prototype) {
          throw new TypeError(`'${name}' called on an object that does not implement interface ${TargetClass.name || 'Target'}.`);
        }
        return methodFn.apply(this, args);
      }
    };
    const fn = holder[name];
    try { Object.defineProperty(fn, 'length', { value: length, configurable: true }); } catch (_) {}
    customToStringMap.set(fn, `function ${name}() { [native code] }`);
    patchedFunctions.add(fn);
    return fn;
  };

  const navProto = pageGlobal.Navigator?.prototype || Object.getPrototypeOf(nav);
  const NavClass = pageGlobal.Navigator || Object;

  function applyIdentity(p) {
    const defineNavGetter = (prop, getter) => {
      try { delete nav[prop]; } catch (_) {}
      try { Object.defineProperty(navProto, prop, { get: getter, configurable: true, enumerable: true }); } catch (_) {}
    };

    if (p.userAgent) {
      defineNavGetter('userAgent', makeNativeGetter('userAgent', () => p.userAgent, NavClass));
      defineNavGetter('appVersion', makeNativeGetter('appVersion', () => p.userAgent.replace(/^Mozilla\//u, ''), NavClass));
    }
    const plat = p.platform || (p.os === 'windows' ? 'Win32' : p.os === 'macos' ? 'MacIntel' : p.os === 'linux' ? 'Linux x86_64' : 'Linux armv8l');
    defineNavGetter('platform', makeNativeGetter('platform', () => plat, NavClass));
    defineNavGetter('vendor', makeNativeGetter('vendor', () => p.vendor || 'Google Inc.', NavClass));

    const uaData = nav.userAgentData;
    if (uaData && typeof uaData === 'object') {
      const targetUaProto = Object.getPrototypeOf(uaData) || uaData;
      const UaClass = pageGlobal.NavigatorUAData || Object;
      const brands = Array.isArray(p.brands) ? p.brands.map(b => ({ brand: b.brand, version: b.version })) : [];
      const isMobile = Boolean(p.mobile || p.os === 'android');
      const chPlatform = p.os === 'windows' ? 'Windows' : p.os === 'macos' ? 'macOS' : p.os === 'linux' ? 'Linux' : p.os === 'android' ? 'Android' : 'Unknown';

      try {
        Object.defineProperty(targetUaProto, 'brands', { get: makeNativeGetter('brands', () => Object.freeze(brands.map(b => ({ ...b }))), UaClass), configurable: true, enumerable: true });
        Object.defineProperty(targetUaProto, 'mobile', { get: makeNativeGetter('mobile', () => isMobile, UaClass), configurable: true, enumerable: true });
        Object.defineProperty(targetUaProto, 'platform', { get: makeNativeGetter('platform', () => chPlatform, UaClass), configurable: true, enumerable: true });

        const he = p.highEntropy || {};
        const fullList = Array.isArray(he.fullVersionList) ? he.fullVersionList.map(b => ({ brand: b.brand, version: b.version })) : brands;
        const getHeValues = function(hints) {
          if (!Array.isArray(hints)) return Promise.reject(new TypeError("Failed to execute 'getHighEntropyValues': parameter 1 is not iterable."));
          const res = { brands: brands.map(b => ({ ...b })), mobile: isMobile, platform: chPlatform };
          for (const h of hints) {
            if (h === 'architecture') res.architecture = he.architecture || 'x86';
            else if (h === 'bitness') res.bitness = he.bitness || '64';
            else if (h === 'model') res.model = he.model || '';
            else if (h === 'platformVersion') res.platformVersion = he.platformVersion || '15.0.0';
            else if (h === 'fullVersionList') res.fullVersionList = fullList.map(b => ({ ...b }));
            else if (h === 'wow64') res.wow64 = Boolean(he.wow64);
            else if (h === 'formFactors') res.formFactors = Array.isArray(he.formFactors) ? [...he.formFactors] : (isMobile ? ['Mobile'] : ['Desktop']);
          }
          return Promise.resolve(res);
        };
        Object.defineProperty(targetUaProto, 'getHighEntropyValues', { value: makeNativeMethod('getHighEntropyValues', 1, getHeValues, UaClass), writable: true, configurable: true, enumerable: true });
        Object.defineProperty(targetUaProto, 'toJSON', { value: makeNativeMethod('toJSON', 0, () => ({ brands: brands.map(b => ({ ...b })), mobile: isMobile, platform: chPlatform }), UaClass), writable: true, configurable: true, enumerable: true });
      } catch (_) {}
    }
  }

  } catch (_) {}
